EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A. 
 
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
3 
 
Statements of the Executives of the 
Group 
Dear Shareholders,  
 
The previous year was a very demanding one, after the 
unprecedented health crisis that hit our entire society. The 
consequences of the pandemic are countless and various and 
have impacted the public health and the economy.  
Since the first day of the appearance of Covid-19 in Greece, 
European Reliance stood next to the society and the State and 
acted as a socially responsible citizen justifying the trust of our 
insured over the past 43 years. 
 
Among other actions, we supported the Health Care System of 
Greece, providing free insurance coverage to the vehicles of 
Mobile Health Units, we participated in the scheme “Special 
Purpose Mobile Health Units” of the National Organization for 
Public Health, we donated technological equipment to schools 
and promoted many other actions that we continue with 
steady paces up to the present day. 
 
Having as primary objective the assurance of the health of the 
employees and insurance agents, we strictly applied all health 
care measures, as imposed by the State, and at the same time 
we supported with all means our people, in order to adjust to 
the new reality, as created by the pandemic. 
 
In this difficult and uncertain environment, European 
Reliance managed to further strengthen the relationships of 
trust with its Insured, presenting total income increase and 
increasing the market share at 5.7%, comparing to 5.2% in 
2019, whereas at the same time the Insurance Market 
presented 3.7 % decrease.  
Referring to the financial results of 2020, we should mention 
the following: 
• 
The Group’s Total Income amounted to € 236.8 mil., 
vs. 225.3 mil. in 2019 presenting 5.1% increase. 
• 
The total Assets increased by 5.6% reaching € 511.9 
mil.  
• 
The Total Equity increased by 8.7% at € 153.6 mil.  
• 
The Insurance Provisions increased by 3.4% at € 312.9 
mil.  
• 
The pre-tax Profit of the Group amounted to € 24.8 
mil., presenting 10.6% increase, comparing to 2019. 
• 
The Company’s Solvency Ratio strengthened by 10.6 
percentage points and amounted to 174.6%, a fact 
that proves the credibility and solvency of the 
Company. 
The increase in the financial figures of European Reliance is the 
result of collective effort for the total of employees and 
administrative cohesion in all management levels. For this 
reason, I would like to express my gratitude to the Human 
Resources and Employees of the Company, for the excellent 
results of 2020 and mostly for their contribution in the 
nomination of the values of the Company, which are not other 
than integrity, consistency, industriousness and prudent 
management. 
 
Our extended experience in combination with the highly 
trained executive personnel and the sales network, gives us the 
opportunity to succeed even in the most difficult periods. We 
maintain our positivity for the future and do our best to 
achieve the strategic and financial objectives. 
 
I wish to all you wholeheartedly happy reading and I hope 2021 
will be a creative and luminous year for all of us.  
 
 
Christos Georgakopoulos 
Chief Executive Officer 
 
 
 
 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A  
 
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
 
Dear Shareholders, 
 
Covid-19 pandemic was the milestone of the previous year, when we were all called to face unprecedented 
challenges, not only in business but in social level. European Reliance managed to stay close to its people and the 
society, in an especially crucial moment and to continue its upward and profitable course. 
 
More specifically, the gross written premiums and related income of the company amounted in 2020 to € 223.3 
mil., versus € 212.4 mil. in 2019, presenting 5.1% increase. The greatest increase was noted in the Life Sector by 
13.5%, following the Other Non-Life Sectors with 2.0% increase and the Motor Sector with 1.4% increase. 
 
The Profit of the Insurance Operations of the Group amounted to € 46.8 mil., vs. € 42.9 mil. in 2019, presenting 
9.0% increase. It is worth noting that all Sectors of the Company (Motor, Life and Other Non-Life) continued their 
profitability in 2020, proving the Company’s ability to create continuous and consistent operational profitability in 
the insurance operations.  
The above figures verify the relationship of trust between European Reliance and its insured and the high quality 
of the provided services, even in a difficult and volatile financial environment.  
 
It is a fact that European Reliance constantly invests in the upgrade and modernization of the provided services, 
with sole purpose to respond fully to the modern requirements of the Greek society. At the same time, European 
Reliance is the only insurance company in Greece, certified with 4 international ISO standards -Quality of Services 
-Information Security, -Energy Management - Compliance, a fact that proves our constant commitment to the 
excellent customer service and Sustainable Development. 
In February 2020, European Reliance proceeded to a strategic and innovative investment for the Greek Insurance 
Market, which is directly related to the need for alternative investments in the field of Alternative Energy Resources 
and the energy Standards ISO 50001:2011 that the Group receives since 2019. More specifically, AEM Energy 
Systems from now on belongs to the Group European Reliance and is active in the field of energy production, 
through the use of photovoltaic power stations. This investment not only strengthens the Group as a total, but 
contributes to the protection of the environment and the preservation of the natural resources for the future 
generations. Our objective is the continuous development of the Group in a sustainable environment, and this has 
been incorporated in our business plan. 
Apart from the quantitative figure, European Reliance puts special emphasis on the qualitative characteristics and 
figures. The Group, for another year, performed numerous Corporate Social Responsibility actions and stood by 
the insurance agents and employees, in a year that the Community needed us more than ever. Continuing the 
actions of 2019 and identifying the efforts of the Company’s executives, the Board of Directors proposes to the 
Ordinary General Meeting of Shareholders the distribution of profit to the personnel from the profit of fiscal year 
2020, equal to € 806 thous.  
European Reliance increases every year its financial figures and mostly its business image in the Greek Insurance 
Market, proving with actions its work, and facing any possible future challenge, by staying close to its insured and 
the society. 
Nikolaos Chalkiopoulos 
Chairman of the Board of Directors  
Chief Insurance Operation Officer 
  
 
 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A. 
 
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
5 
 
Dear Shareholders, 
 
Since the beginning of 2020, the pandemic had affected our society in a national and international level. 
European Reliance has always set human as its top priority and applied all required health care protection 
measures and actively supported vulnerable social groups and government entities. 
The financial consequences of this health care crisis are expected to be difficult since the estimates refer to 
two-digit recession rates. However, the gradual vaccination of the population, the progressive removal of 
restrictions in transportation and the rebound in tourism are expected to create the first steps for the return 
to normality and the relaunching of business activities as a total.  
In a difficult and uncertain financial environment, our Group managed to increase its pre-tax Profit by 10.6%, 
comparing to the previous fiscal year, at € 24.8 mil., mostly affected by the provision of new and innovative 
digital tools in the Sales Network, and the beginning of new co-operations, with 6.0% increase of the Gross 
Earned Premiums.  
To all of the above, it is worth presenting the actions that took place in 2020 for the benefit of our shareholders. 
More specifically, the Board of Directors of the Company applied carefully the plan for repurchase of Own 
Shares, as approved by the Board of Directors, purchasing by the end of 2020, a total of 812,464 shares. 
According to the plan for repurchase of Own Shares, the shareholders of the Company approved the write-off 
of a total of 910,316 Own Shares, through the Extraordinary General Meeting of  
Shareholders that took place on March 2, 2021. Moreover, regarding fiscal year 2020, the Board of Directors 
proposes to the Ordinary General Meeting of Shareholders the distribution of dividend of a total amount of € 
7,962 thous., which, except for the Own Shares, amounts to € 0.30 per share and it consists the maximum 
dividend amount that has been distributed by our Company to the shareholders over the past 43 years of 
operation. 
We always remain consistent to our values and pay our respect towards all human beings, the society, and the 
environment. We continue to invest in the Human Resources of the Company and support our Insurance 
Agents, which actively contributed to the increase of our Company, offering sufficient guidance, active dialogue 
and all required technological means. We daily strive to achieve the decrease of the environmental footprint 
of the Company and at the same time, we perform all technical projects for the continuous energy upgrade of 
the facilities, and the projects that contribute to the overall digital upgrade of the financial services and 
compliance with the new International Financial Reporting Standards 17 & 9. 
The unprecedented conditions that we successfully managed in 2020, consist a significant experience for the 
continuation of the upward trend of our Group and the assurance of its personnel, insured and all interested 
parties.  
 
Stefanos Verzovitis 
Vice-Chairman of the Board of Directors 
Chief Financial Officer

Introduction  
 
 
 
 
Dear Shareholders, 
 
According to the provisions of articles 150-154 of Law 4548/2018, the provisions of Law 
3556/2007, article 4, paragraphs 2c, 6, 7 & 8, the provision of Law 4548/2018, the decision 
of the Capital Market Commission No. 7/448/11.10.2007, article 2 and the Company’s 
Article of Association, we submit to you the Annual Management Report of the Board of 
Directors for the time period from 01/01/2020 to 31/12/2020.  
 
The present report contains general information on the financial position and the financial 
results of the Group and the Parent Company “European Reliance General Insurance Co. 
S.A.”, the financial information that aim to inform the shareholders and the investors, the 
overall course and changes that occurred in the fiscal year from 01/01/2020 to 
31/12/2020, including the description of important facts that took place during this fiscal 
period and their impact on the financial statements of this period.  
 
Moreover, the present report describes the major risks and uncertainties and the System 
of Corporate Governance, as well as the expected course and development and presents 
the transactions performed between the Company and the Group with the related 
undertakings.  
 
Furthermore, in Section G of the present report and the paragraph of the Non-Financial 
Information of the present Report, we present the Group’s non-financial information 
according to L. 4403/2016 and 4548/2018. 
 
 
The Chairman of the Board of Directors 
 
 
 
Nikolaos Chalkiopoulos

European Reliance Group of Companies  
EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE COMPANY S.A. (S.A. Reg. No. 12855/05/Β/86/35), General 
Electronic Commercial Registry -G.E.M.I. No. 322801000), was established in 1977. The Company’s 
objective, according to Art. 4 of its Articles of Association, is to provide all kinds of insurance and 
reinsurance coverage, except for credit and guarantee insurance. Its headquarters are in 274 Kifisias 
Avenue, P.C. 15232, Chalandri, Athens, Greece. 
The Group includes the following companies: 
• European Reliance General Insurance Co. S.A. (parent company)  
• Alter Ego S.A.  
• European Reliance Asset Management M.F.M.S.A.  
• Reliance Single-Member Insurance Agents S.A.  
• AEM Energy Systems Private Company 
• Partner Hotel S.A. 
European Reliance General Insurance Co. S.A. is governed by the provisions of L. 4548/2018 on the 
“Reformation of the legislation of Société Anonymes” (to replace Law 2190/1920) and the special 
provisions of L. 4364/2016, as applicable and in force. Moreover, the Company is governed, by any 
provision, Law or decision of the Capital Market Commission and the Athens Stock Exchange. The 
Company’s supervisory authority is the Private Insurance Supervision Committee of the Bank of Greece 
and the Capital Market Commission, as a Company listed in the Athens Stock Exchange. 
 
Core values  
rinciples 
Integrity, diligence, industriousness and prudent management are the key and inviolable principles that 
characterize European Reliance General Insurance S.A. These principles are applied in the Company's 
relations with any transacting party: insured, employees, shareholders and third parties. The Company 
retains its philosophy to stay close to the insured, treat them with respect at difficult moments, serve 
them with constancy and loyalty. This philosophy characterizes the Management of the Company and its 
employees and is emphasized in the Company’s slogan "Pays immediately". European Reliance always 
stays close to every insured and provides its services fast, polite, willingly, and always with a smile. 
Our Vision 
 The creation and development of an organization that will satisfy the needs of all citizens in times 
of need and fulfill all their desires. 
 The creation and development of an organization that will provide a permanent and stable working 
position to employees, that will help them fulfill their financial, administrative and social ambitions. 
 The creation and development of an organization that will reward the investors and the 
shareholders for their trust. 
 The creation of a powerful organization with strong financial, emotional and intellectual assets. 
 
 
 
Prudent management

European Reliance has proven all its years of operation, that is respects and honors its insured, 
shareholders and every transacting party with the Company, as a company that manages their interests 
with consistency and responsibility. 
 
Strategic goals 
 The continuous improvement of quality and range of the services offered to every citizen - 
customer. 
 The creation of a society of satisfied individuals, the people of European Reliance General 
Insurance Co. S.A. - whose satisfaction will derive from their participation in decision-making and 
from the company’s financial results. 
 The impact and daily increase of the number of customers and market share, with final objective 
to reach the first position in the market. 
 The creation and development of new business activities for the fastest implementation of the 
fiscal and administrative ambitions of the Company’s executives.

Significant Milestones  
 
Year 
Event 
1977 
The Company was established by a team of 19 life Insurance Agents. 
1983 
Construction of our first building of 3,500 m² on Kifisias Avenue. 
1986 
Establishment of the subsidiary company “ALTER EGO S.A.”, a center for the support of 
the Quality of life insurance schemes, a new insurance scheme firstly introduced to the 
insurance industry by European Reliance. 
1990 
Establishment of the Company European Reliance Mutual Funds Management S.A., one 
of the first Société Anonymes in Mutual Funds Management. 
1995 
Construction of a building of 16,000 m², next to the first building on Kifisias Avenue, 
that is a point of reference, of special value and a stable source of rental income. 
1997 
The company is publicly listed on the Athens Stock Exchange. European Reliance is the 
first insurance company over the past 34 years to achieve that. 
2002 
ISO 9001:2000 Certification for the total of our Company’s services. European Reliance 
is the first company to receive this certification and maintains it up to this day with the 
new ISO 9001:2015. 
2005 
Since 01/01/2005, European Reliance, as a company listed on the Athens Stock 
Exchange implements the IFRS. According to a study conducted by Ernst and Young, 
European Reliance was the only insurance company that improved its equity, after the 
application of the IFRS. 
2007 
Agreement between European Reliance and Piraeus Bank, where Piraeus Bank acquired 
30% of European Reliance’s share capital, through a capital increase and resignation 
from old shares. 
2011 
Successful participation in the Pan-european stress test of insurance companies, by 
fulfilling all assessment criteria. 
2012 
Tripling of profit at € 11.6 mil. vs. € 4 mil in 2011, despite the third consecutive year of 
recession in the insurance market. 
2015 
increase of profitability at € 16.1 mil. 
2016 
The company is ready for the new “Solvency II” framework. The European Bank for 
Reconstruction and Development (EBRD) acquires 15% of the share capital of European 
Reliance General Insurance Co. S.A. and the total investment portfolio of the Group 
exceeds the amount of € 300 mil.

Subsidiaries 
 
European Reliance Asset Management M.F.M.S.A. 
 European Reliance ASSET MANAGEMENT M.F.M S.A. was established in 1990 and 
specializes in mutual fund management. The company is active in the sectors of 
Portfolio Management, Corporate Finance, Venture Capital and develops 
Investment Banking for the provision of investment services. The purpose of the 
Company is to build long-term relationships with the customers that will be based 
on trust. The assets under management on 31/12/2020 amounted to € 472 mil. 
and the total turnover amounted to € 1.0 mil. The Company operates according to 
three main pillars: 
 
1. Specialization 
Our managers and top executives are highly trained, have extensive professional experience and 
knowledge of the domestic and foreign capital and money markets and are able to meet the special 
requirements of every investor. 
2. Safety 
The internal audit procedures and the proper implementation of the applicable legislation protect our 
customers’ interests and provide complete transparency at all stages of cash management. All invested 
assets and securities are deposited to the custodian, are monitored in separate accounts for every 
customer and are kept at the Company’s Registry. In addition, our Company participates in a scheme for 
compensation of investors with title “Investment Guarantee Fund”, is a member of the Hellenic Fund and 
Asset Management Association and is supervised by the Hellenic Capital Market Commission. 
3. Personalized Services 
Credibility and constant communication rounds are the key factors for a successful and long-term 
partnership with every customer. The Company, via the Account Manager services, offers to customers 
high-quality investment services, keeps them updated on the developments and prospects of the 
domestic and foreign capital and money markets, provides direct customer service and adapts their needs 
to the new market data, with special emphasis on the quality of the services and the human factor. The 
Company consists of a team of experienced agents and acknowledged executives, that apply all modern 
technologies and can guarantee for the company’s effectiveness. 
 
Alter Ego Facilities Management S.A. 
ALTER EGO Facilities Management S.A. is one of the largest 
companies that provides integrated Facility Management 
services. The Company operates throughout Greece, providing its 
services to leading Greek and multinational companies and its 
active customer base includes more than 65 private companies. 
Since its first establishment, in 1986, Alter Ego Facilities 
Management S.A. pursues long-term business relationships with 
customers, through the customer-oriented approach, based on 
the win-win model. 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A. 
 
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
17 
 
 
ALTER EGO S.A. has more than 600 employees and manages facilities of customers that exceed a total of 
1.000.000 m². The Company specializes in the administration of professional real estate (office buildings, 
hospitals, shopping centers, chain stores, industrial and storage facilities, critical facilities, meeting rooms, 
hotels, etc.) and its services are in compliance with the International Standards for Quality, Occupational 
Health and Safety and Environmental Management. Moreover, it has been selected by major international 
companies in the fields of Real Estate, Property Management and Facilities Management as one of their 
main partners in the Greek insurance market and is a founding member of the independent Hellenic 
Facility Management Association. The Company’s Total Turnover in 2020 amounted to € 12.5 mil. 
 
The main objective of the Company is to further strengthen its position in the Greek market. For the 
achievement of this goal, the Company has invested in human resources, creating an excellent team of 
professionals, and in new technologies by purchasing high-tech equipment, management software and 
create infrastructures of the latest technology. Over the past decade, ALTER EGO tries to provide 
innovative services to its customers and therefore is oriented towards the provision of integrated facilities 
management solutions by presenting new innovative services. Offering single– stop solutions to 
companies that want to control their operating costs, ALTER EGO S.A. manages to add value to its 
partnerships. Since 2017, Alter Ego is the co-founder of Partner Hotel S.A., a leading company in the 
provision of comprehensive housekeeping services in large tourism operations. 
 
The vision of ALTER EGO S.A. has remained unchanged over the years: 
· 
The creation and development of an organization that will be a preferable partner of the leading 
companies in Greece for Facilities Management. 
· 
The creation and development of an organization that will provide a permanent and stable working 
position to employees, that will help them fulfill their financial, administrative and social ambitions. 
· 
The creation and development of an organization that will reward the investors and the 
shareholders for their trust. 
Reliance Single-Member Insurance Agents S.A.  
Reliance Single Member S.A. is an insurance broker, founded in 2012, non-active company. 
 
AEM Energy Systems Private Company  
The Company AEM Energy Systems is active in the field of energy production, through the use of 
photovoltaic power stations. On February 2020, the Group completed the acquisition of the company 
AEM Energy Systems that owns a photovoltaic station in the Region of Thessaly, of nominal value 0.5 
ΜWh, which is connected in the medium voltage network of the Public Power Station.

Statement of Corporate Governance 
According to article 152 of L. 4548/2018, the Annual Management Report of the Board of Directors of 
European Reliance General Insurance Co. S.A. (hereinafter “the Company”) prepares a Statement of 
Corporate Governance for fiscal year 2020. The reporting date of the Statement is 31/12/2020. 
 
Corporate Governance refers to the total of principles and practices adopted by European Reliance in 
order to ensure its performance and the interest of the insured, shareholders and stakeholders. The 
Company’s System of Governance is based on the principles of Corporate Governance, to achieve the 
Company’s prudent and effective management, through the efficient utilization of all available productive 
resources and transparency in all corporate functions to preserve the long-term interest of the 
shareholders. Corporate governance has a positive contribution to the Company’s performance as it 
focuses on the following sections: 
 
• The Board of Directors and the executive Management in terms of the rate of the independent 
members, the composition of the BoD Committees, the Board assessment procedures, the 
responsibilities of the Chairman and the Chief Executive Officer. 
• The shareholders’ rights (one share-one vote principle) 
• The assurance of sufficient transparency and proper audit procedures with the contribution of the 
four independent Key Functions (Risk Management, Internal Audit, Compliance, Actuarial 
Function). 
 
Within the framework of Corporate Governance, European Reliance General Insurance Co. S.A., aims to 
implement best practices in the functions of the Organization, including also voluntary commitments, as 
a result of its business ethics. European Reliance, as a Société Anonyme (S.A.) listed in the Athens Stock 
Exchange implements the principles and practices of corporate governance according to the Greek 
Legislation and partially applies the Hellenic Corporate Governance Code on the listed companies, as 
published in the official website of the Athens Stock Exchange. (https://www.athexgroup.gr/web/guest/esed-
hellenic-cgc).  
 
Moreover, the four independent key Functions of Internal Audit, Risk Management, Actuarial Function 
and Compliance contribute to the best possible function towards the legislative environment of Solvency 
II Directive.  
The philosophy and culture that govern the Statement of Corporate Governance are imprinted in a series 
of regulations and policies, such as the Internal Regulation of Corporate Governance and Operations, the 
Corporate Code of Conduct, the Compliance Policy, the Internal Audit Policy, the Policy for the Prevention 
and Management of Cases of Conflict of Interests, the Remuneration Policy, Communications Policy, etc. 
 
Deviations from the Code of Corporate Governance 
The Company, depending on the case, deviates or does not apply certain Principles of the Code of 
Corporate Governance as a total, which do not consist legislative regulation, but best practices, that are 
imprinted as follows: 
  
 
 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A. 
 
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
19 
 
The Board of Directors and its members: 
• Appointment of an independent Vice-Chairman, when the Chairman is an executive member, 
and non-participation of the Chairman in the individual assessment. 
• The maximum term of office of the Board members is the time-period of 4 years, whereas the 
term of office of the Board members of European Reliance is 5 years. 
• Periodic meetings of the non-executive Board members, without the presence of executive 
Board members, for the assessment of the performance and the determination of the 
remuneration. 
• Adherence of the minutes for the discussions and decisions of the BoD Committees by the 
Secretary: Every Committee has a different secretary. 
Remuneration of BoD members: 
• Association of the remuneration of the non-executive members with the time that they devote 
for the exercise of their duties. 
• Determination of the remuneration of the BoD executive members, taking into consideration the 
remuneration of the employees of the Company and the Group. 
 
The Board of Directors 
 
The Board of Directors is the highest administrative body of the Company. It shapes the Company’s 
strategic and growth policy and supervises the Company's asset management. The Board makes decisions, 
monitors all activities of the Company and constantly supervises the executives of the Company that are 
delegated with executive responsibilities by the organization chart or by the Board of Directors. 
 
The powers and responsibilities of the Board of Directors of the Company are described in the Article of 
Association. 
 
Any matter related to any type of remuneration of the Company's Executive Directors, Head Officers of 
the four Key Functions, and the overall remuneration policy of the Company, are included in the 
competencies of the Corporate Governance, Remuneration and Nomination Committee. 
 
The Board of Directors consists of nine (9) members, executive or non-executive. The executive members 
are responsible for the Company's daily management issues. The non-executive members are responsible 
for promoting corporate issues. The number of the non-executive members of the Board should, based 
on the Company’s Article of Association, should not be less than the 1/3 of the total BoD members and if 
there is a fraction, it should be rounded to the next integer. 
 
There must be at least 2 independent members among the non-executive members. The identity of the 
executive or non-executive BoD members is defined by the Board of Directors. The independent members 
are appointed by the General Meeting of Shareholders. If the Board of Directors elects a temporary 
member by the first General Meeting of the Shareholders, as a deputy of another independent member 
that resigned, was absent, or for any reason was deprived of the rights, the elected member must also be 
independent. During their term of office, the independent non-executive Board members should not own 
shares representing over 0.5% of the Company’s share capital and must not be engaged in a dependency 
relationship with the Company, or with related persons, within the meaning of Art. 4, par. 1 of Law 
3016/2002.

General Meeting of Shareholders of Companies that will take place till July 2021, a new Board of Directors 
will be elected. 
The management of European Reliance has not yet approved a discrete Diversity Policy, since with 
corporate Policies and Procedures, as well as the Corporate Code of Conduct, takes all measures for the 
enrichment and strengthening of the diversity of the administrative and supervisory bodies of the 
Company, and in general the human resources of the Company. 
 
Within this framework, the selection of the persons that consist the administrative and supervisory bodies 
is based on their professional abilities and credentials and are not evaluated based on the race, gender, 
color, citizenship or ethnic origin, class, religion, age, disability, family status, sexual orientation or gender 
identity, political considerations or any other status that is protected by the applicable legislation. 
 
The existence of diversity is especially important, since when there is diversity on the skills, competences, 
experiences and personalities of the Board members and in-house operations, then the role and 
contribution of the human resources is strengthened.

Other Administrative Bodies 
 
The General Meeting of Shareholders 
 
The General Meeting of Shareholders is the highest administrative body of the Company. It is 
entitled to make decisions on business affairs, unless it is otherwise specified in the Article of 
Association. 
 
The General Meeting of Shareholders is convened by the Board of Directors and arranges meetings 
in the Company’s headquarters or in the region of another municipality within the prefecture of the 
headquarters or another neighbor municipality of the headquarters, at least on an annual basis, 
and the latest within six months since the end of the fiscal year, and extraordinarily, if this is found 
necessary. 
 
With the exception of the repetitive meetings, the invitation of the shareholders for the General 
Meeting of Shareholders must be published twenty (20) days before the day of the meeting. The 
complete text of the invitation is published within the above deadline in the Company’s website 
and in a method that ensures its fast and non-discriminatory access. 
Any shareholder that can prove his/her identity at the date of the General Meeting of Shareholders 
is entitled to participate. Shareholders that are legal entities may participate in the General Meeting 
via their proxies. 
 
The General Meeting of Shareholders is in quorum and meets on the subjects on the agenda, when 
the participants are shareholders, or their proxies are equal to 1/5 of the paid up share capital, 
except for the cases that the Law explicitly requires increased quorum, and the shareholders or 
their representatives must be equal to 1/2 of the paid up share capital. 
 
If there is no quorum, the General Meeting of Shareholders convenes again within twenty (20) days 
within the date of the canceled meeting, after an invitation disclosed at least ten (10) days before. 
In the repetitive meeting, the General Meeting of Shareholders is in quorum and convenes promptly 
on the subjects of the daily agenda, regardless of the represented part of the paid up share capital. 
A new invitation is not required, if the first invitation includes the place and time of the repetitive 
meeting, provided that the time period of at least five (5) working days interferes between the 
canceled meeting and the repetitive meeting.  
 
The General Meeting of Shareholders takes decisions with the total majority of the represented 
votes. The decisions provided for in paragraph 3 and 4 of article 130 of L. 4548/2018 are taken into 
consideration per case, with the majority of the 1/2 and 1/3 (or 1/5) of the votes represented in the 
meeting. 
 
The General Meeting of Shareholders decides on all submitted subjects and is the only 
administrative body responsible to decide for: 
• 
Amendments in the Articles of Association. As amendments we consider the ordinary or 
extraordinary increase and decrease of the share capital. 
• 
Appointment of the Board members and the Auditors. 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
26 
 
• 
Approval of the total management according to article 108 of L. 4548/2018 and exemption 
of the auditors. 
• 
Approval of the annual and consolidated financial statements. 
• 
Distribution of the annual profit. 
• 
Mergers, dissolution, conversion, revitalization, extension of the liquidation of the 
Company and 
• 
Appointment of the liquidators. 
 
The Shareholders of the Company representing at least 1/20 of the paid up share capital are entitled 
to request the audit of the Company by a competent Court of the region where the Company has 
its headquarters.  
 
The Shareholders of the Company representing 1/5 of the paid up share capital are entitled to 
request by the competent Court the audit of the Company, if the Company does not apply sound 
and prudent management in its business activities. This provision does not apply when the 
requested minority is represented in the Company’s Board of Directors. 
 
The shareholders ought to prove their shareholder’s identity and the number of shares owned, 
when they exercise their rights. To the degree that the Company owns listed shares on a regulated 
market, the shareholder may prove the identity via any legal mean and based on the update that 
the Company receives by the Central Securities Depository, if it provides registry services or 
otherwise via the participating and registered in the Central Securities Depository intermediaries.

2. Audit Committee  
 
The Audit Committee consists of at least three (3) non-executive Board members and most of them 
are independent. All members are appointed by the General Meeting of Shareholders, have 
adequate knowledge of the Company’s sector of activities and at least one of the members has 
sufficient proven knowledge in accounting and auditing matters. The President of the Audit 
Committee must be an independent, non-executive member, appointed by the Committee’s 
members. The project of the Committee is the supervision of the Internal Auditors of the Company, 
the overview of the published financial information, the audit and evaluation of the systems of 
internal audit, the assessment and coordination of the audit function and procedures, based on the 
applicable legislation that governs the Company, and the recommendation to the General Meeting 
of Shareholders for the selection of the Certified Public Accountants for every fiscal year. 
 
The Audit Committee operates according to a special regulation, which is part of the Company’s 
Internal Regulation of Operation. The Audit Committee’s regulation is approved and revised by the 
Board of Directors. The Audit Committee meets at least four times every year. 
 
The Audit Committee consists of the following members:

4. Risk Management Committee

The Board assigns to the Risk Management Committee (RMC) responsibilities relevant to the 
management of risks so that all forms of risks are effectively monitored, including the operational 
and insurance risks, and so that their integrated control, specialized treatment and required 
coordination for the insurance company are ensured.

6. Product Oversight and Governance Committee  
 
The Product Oversight and Governance Committee was established in February 2019 and aims at 
the Company’s compliance with the requirements of L. 4583 for the integration of the Insurance 
Distribution Directive in the Greek Legislation. 
 
The Product Oversight and Governance Committee operates in order to implement the principles 
of product oversight and governance in the Company’s operations, within the framework of internal 
functions and operations of the Company, the product design, introduction of the product in the 
market, product monitoring and review throughout its lifespan. 
 
The main objectives of the function of the Product Oversight and Governance Committee, which 
are its main responsibilities, are the following:  
• 
Coordination of the design of new products or modifications in the existing products  
• 
Assurance of the proper execution of the product implementation plan 
• 
Recommendation to the Board of Directors of the approval of new products or the 
amendment of existing products 
• 
Proposes to the Board of Directors corrective actions, in case the existing products deviate 
from the interests, objectives and characteristics of the target-market for which they have 
been designed. 
• 
Monitors the fiscal performance and capital requirement of the products comparing to the 
objectives set in their import to the market and provides recommendations for 
improvements, when necessary.  
 
The Product Oversight & Governance Committee consists of the following members:

7. Complaints Committee 
 
The Company’s Board of Directors puts special emphasis on the received complaints and for their 
proper management has established a Complaints Management Policy, special procedures and the 
Complaints Committee. The Complaints Committee consists of the Chief Insurance Operation

Ability and Ethics Requirements 
 
In order to ensure the Company's sound and prudent management by the appropriate persons and 
enhance the insured and stakeholders' protection, the Company established and applies a Policy 
and Procedures for the Assessment of persons responsible in the key functions. The Policy has been 
established in order to define the principles and criteria based on which the responsible persons 
will be assessed for their ability to adequately perform their tasks. 
 
The term “responsible persons” refers to the members of the Board of Directors, the Appointed 
Actuary, the Risk Manager, the Internal Audit Officer, the Compliance Officer, any person with 
special duties based on the applicable legislation, as well as any person with major auditing or 
administrative role, or with duties that may essentially affect the Company’s management. 
 
The responsible persons are assessed for: 
a. Honesty, integrity and good reputation. 
b. Professional training and market experience. 
c. The adequacy of their knowledge in relation to the tasks to be undertaken. 
d. The non-existence of incompatibility or conflict of interest with the tasks to be undertaken. 
 
The appropriateness and credibility of the Board members is annually reviewed with the Self-
Assessment Procedure, under the supervision of the Corporate Governance, Remuneration and 
Nomination Committee The appropriateness and credibility of the rest of the “Responsible Persons” 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
31 
 
is annually reviewed within the context of the Annual Assessment of Performance and Target 
Setting of the Division of Human Resources. 
 
Independent Key Functions 
 
Actuarial Function 
 
The Actuarial Function coordinates the calculation of Technical Provisions, gives opinions on the 
Underwriting and Reinsurance Policies and contributes to the Risk Management. 
 
Technical Provisions  
The Actuarial Function calculates the technical provisions of the Company by implementing special 
methodologies and procedures. Ensures the appropriateness of the methods and models used, as 
well as the assumptions and assertions on which the calculation of the technical provisions is based. 
For this reason, the Actuarial Function develops various calculation scenarios, justifies variations 
between two successive valuation dates and compares the calculations with the empirical 
observations. 
Evaluates the sufficiency and quality of data used in the calculation and makes proposals for 
changes in internal procedures for the improvement of the data. 
 
Opinion on the Underwriting Policy 
The Actuarial Function provides its opinion on the Underwriting Policy.  
Assesses the sufficiency levels of the premiums, using the current experience through regular 
audits. Reports cases where the premiums do not cover the expenses, claims and guarantees. 
 
Opinion on the Reinsurance Policy 
The Actuarial Function provides its opinion on the Reinsurance Policy, reporting cases where the 
reinsurance policy does not cover the claims.  
It evaluates the consistency of the Reinsurance Policy with the Underwriting Policy and with the 
Company’s risk appetite. It also evaluates the reinsurance cost. 
 
Contribution to the Risk Management 
The Actuarial Function contributes to the risk management in the creation and management of 
models for the quantification of insurance risks. It makes recommendations on the limit of values 
for the insurance risks.  
Calculates the largest part of the Risk ratios used in the Risk Management Reports. It participates in 
the procedures of risk identification and estimation and in the total solvency requirements for the 
preparation of the ORSA report, the Asset & Liability Management (ALM), and the techniques for 
the mitigation of the underwriting risk. 
 
The Actuarial Function prepares a written analytical report annually to inform the Management and 
the Company's Board of Directors via the Risk Management Committee. The report analyzes all 
undertaken projects and their results. It also identifies potential deficiencies and omissions and 
proposes methods for their correction.  
 
 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
32 
 
Compliance 
Compliance is defined as the obligation of the Company’s Board of Directors, the Management and 
the Personnel to adjust and operate according to the applicable legislative and regulatory 
framework, the decision and guidelines of EIOPA, the Company's Articles of Association, the Internal 
Regulations and the Codes of Conduct in the performance of their tasks, for the mitigation of the 
risk of financial loss, the promotion of the Company's good reputation and protection of its 
credibility and status. 
 
For this reason, the Company’s Board of Directors has approved the Compliance Policy. The 
implementation of this Policy implies the continuous compliance of the Policy with the current 
applicable legislative and regulatory framework, the prevention and deterrence of risks related to 
Compliance and is a major obligation for all functions, bodies and employees of all ranks of the 
Company. 
 
The Compliance Function reports to the Board of Directors, through the Corporate Governance, 
Remuneration and Nomination Committee, and operationally reports directly to the Company’s 
Management, and the General Divisions, for any significant violation of the regulatory framework 
detected in its business operations. The Compliance Function is administratively independent and 
submits reports to the Board of Directors on matters relevant to the Board's responsibilities, at least 
annually, in a way that ensures its independence and avoids conflict of interest. The Compliance 
Function has the opportunity to access all information required for the fulfillment of its objective. 
 
Internal Audit Function 
 
The Internal Audit Function is administratively independent from functions with executive duties 
and from functions that are responsible for performing or finalizing transactions. It operatively 
reports to the BoD through the Audit Committee and administratively to the Chief Executive Officer. 
 
The Internal Audit operates in accordance with the “Internal Audit Statute”, as approved by the 
Board of Directors, and the International Standards for the Professional Practice of Internal 
Auditing. 
 
The Internal Audit Function reviews and submits recommendations for improvement on the 
corporate procedures of the organization. The audit that is conducted by the Internal Audit 
Function, as well as the proposals submitted for improvement, do not constitute executive work, 
neither do they release the Board of Directors (Audit Committee) from its legal obligations (Statute 
of the Audit Committee). 
  
The Company's Board of Directors is responsible for the development and implementation of an 
effective Internal Control System, which shall provide to the Shareholders and the Company’s 
Management reasonable assurance on the achievement of the following goals: 
 
• The effectiveness and productivity of the corporate functions 
• The credibility of the financial statements, 
• The Compliance with the applicable laws, regulations and provisions. 
 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
33 
 
The structure of the Internal Control System of European Reliance General Insurance Co S.A. is 
based on the International COSO standard (Committee of Sponsoring Organizations of Treadway 
Commission). The COSO framework is globally recognized for its contribution on issues of corporate 
governance, business ethics, internal control, business risk management, fraud deterrence and 
reports. 
COSO is a great tool for the estimation of the effectiveness of the Internal Control System of an 
Organization. 
 
Internal Control System 
 
Main Components of the Internal Control System (ICS) 
 
The ICS Components include five sectors:  
• Control Environment  
• Risk Assessment  
• Control Activities  
• Information and Communication  
• Monitoring Activities. 
 
Control Environment 
 
The Control Environment consists of standards, procedures and infrastructure that provide the 
bases for the implementation of the Internal Control System to the Organization. The Board of 
Directors and the Management define the significance of the Internal Control System (Tone at the 
top) and the expected ethical standards. 
 
Risk Assessment 
 
Risk assessment includes a dynamic feedback process to identify and analyze the risks regarding the 
achievement of the corporate objectives, by creating a database for risk management. 
Management co-estimates possible changes in the external and internal environment, that may 
affect the ability to achieve corporate objectives.  
 
Control Activities 
 
The control activities are actions that are defined by policies and procedures that contribute and 
assure that the guidelines of the Management are implemented for risk management and the 
achievement of corporate objectives. The control activities are performed at all levels of the 
Organization, in various stages of the corporate objectives and the IT systems. 
 
Information and Communication 
 
The existence of adequate information is a prerequisite for the implementation of all competencies 
deriving from the Internal Control System for the achievement of corporate objectives. The 
communication is conducted inside and outside the Organization and provides all required 
information for the performance of the daily auditing activities. The communication allows the 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
34 
 
personnel to understand the competencies deriving by the Internal Control System and their 
significance for the achievement of the corporate objectives. 
 
Monitoring Activities 
 
The constant assessments and the individual evaluations or their combination are utilized to verify 
whether one of the five components and one of the seventeen principles of the Internal Control 
System are present and properly implemented. The findings are evaluated,and the weaknesses are 
communicated promptly. The significant weaknesses are reported to the Board of Directors and the 
Management of the Company. 
 
Risk Management System 
 
Model of Governance for Risk Management 
 
The Company adopts a model that is based on the three lines of defense for the governance of risk 
management, which has the following structure: 
 
• In the first line of defense there are the operational units (Divisions and Departments), that 
are responsible for the assessment and minimization of risks on a given level of expected 
performance. 
• In the second line of defense there is the Risk Manager who detects, monitors, quantifies 
(where possible), controls the risks and provides for the provision of appropriate 
methodologies and risk management tools. Moreover, the Risk Manager informs the 
competent bodies, namely the Risk Management Committee and the Board of Directors. 
• In the third line of defense, there is the Internal Audit Officer, responsible for the independent 
assessment of the Company’s compliance with the applicable risk management framework 
and its effectiveness. 
 
 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.

Outsourcing 
"Outsourcing" is defined as the agreement of any form between the Company and a service 
provider, whether supervised or not, with which the service provider performs a procedure, 
provides a service or exercises an activity, which otherwise would be performed, provided or 
exercised by the Company itself. 
The Company has not outsourced any of its key functions. However, the Company has entrusted 
the management of the Company’s Investment Portfolio to European Reliance Asset Management

•

•

•

•

• Limitation of cost 
• Focus on the key functions of the Company, 
• Possibility of access to new technologies 
• Release of resources 
• Expertise 
• Quality of services 
• Flexibility in the determination of priorities 
• Better management 
• Effective monitoring of performance

Analysis of the Financial Environment 
The Global and European financial and insurance environment 
According to the Organization for Economic Co‑operation and Development (OECD), the global 
economy decreased by 4.2% in 2020, and in 2021, we expect 4.2% growth rate and in 2022 3.7% 
deceleration. After the second Coronavirus wave that hit Europe and the U.S.A., the Organization 
revised its provisions of September, when 4.5% global decrease was expected, before the 5% recess 
in 2021. 
The Gross Domestic Product is estimated to return at pro-crisis levels by the end of 2021, with a 
positive impact mostly by the strong recovery of China, since the contributions of U.S.A. And Europe 
are assumed to be less. 
 
According to the OECD, the economy of the Eurozone decreased by 7.5% in 2020, and many 
economies closed the fiscal year with double recession, after the new imposition of a banning of 
traffic. The economy of the Eurozone will return to growth in 2021, achieving 3.6% growth rate and 
3.3% growth rate in 2022. 
 
The European insurance market, having as main attempt, the close monitoring of the consequences 
of Covid-19 pandemic, since the early stages of appearance of the crisis, approved an organized 
dialogue between the EIOPA, the European Commission and European insurance agents, through 
Insurance Europe. In order to present the position of the insurance market as to the pandemic, 
Insurance Europe created a document of education character, to inform the European institutions, 
on the extend of the coverage provide by the non-life sector, the gaps in the coverage and the 
options for their restoration. Moreover, Insurance Europe disclosed its position to the European 
Commission, stating the willingness of the sector for further cooperation for the treatment of any 
possible future pandemics, as systemic risks, through a framework that will be based on the 
cooperation of the public and private sector.

According to the last estimates of the Hellenic Statistical Authority, the GDP of the country 
amounted to €168.5 bil. in 2020, versus € 183.6 bil. in 2019, presenting 8.2% decrease. This 
decrease is mainly due to the variations recorded per component of the GDP and are analyzed in 
the following table.

The Greek Insurance Market 
Based on the recent figures of the Hellenic Association of Insurance Companies (HAIC), the total 
gross written premiums amounted to € 3.9 bil., presenting 3.7% decrease, comparing to 2019, 
breaking the upward trend of the past three years.  
 
More specifically, in Life Insurance, the gross written premiums amounted to 1.82 bil. in 2020 
(46.3% of the total insurance premiums, vs. -1.98 bil. in 2019), decreased by 8.0%, comparing to 
2019, and mostly affected by the financial consequences of the Covid-19 pandemic and the 
uncertain fiscal environment.  
 
In Non-Life Insurance, the gross written premiums amounted in 2020 to € 2.11 bil. (53.7% of the 
total) vs. € 2.10 bil. In 2019, presenting 0.4% marginal increase. The gross written premiums for the 
Third Party Liability of Land Vehicles presented 5.7% decrease and the gross written premiums of 
Other Non-Life sectors presented 3.9% increase. 
 
According to the Statistics of the Insurance Companies of December 2020, published by the Bank 
of Greece, the total assets of the insurance companies amounted to € 19,965 mil. in the end of the 
4th quarter of 2020. More specifically, the total deposits of the insurance undertakings increased by 
€ 15 mil. and amounted to € 1,414 mil. In the end of the 4th quarter of 2020. The deposits in credit 
institutions in Greece presenting an increase equal to € 56 mil. And the deposits in credit institutions 
abroad decreased by € 41 mil. The rate of the deposits on the total assets amounted to 7.1% in the 
4th quarter of 2020. 
 
Regarding the Liabilities, the Total Equity increased by € 180 mil. and amounted to € 3,381 mil. in 
the end of the 4th quarter of 2020. The insurance technical provisions increased by € 280 mil. And 
amounted to € 14,887 mil., mostly due to the increase of the Life insurance technical provisions by 
€ 332 mil. The Non-Life technical provisions decreased by € 52 mil. And amounted to € 3,050 mil. 
The 73.1% of the total technical provisions corresponds to Life technical provisions, excluding 
pensions.

Impact of Covid-19 and actions of the Group 
In the beginning of 2020, the insurance undertakings increased their efforts to increase the liquidity 
ratio, provide satisfactory operating profitability and improving the Risk Management Systems, and 
especially within the first two months, the gross written premiums presented an upward trend.  
 
The outbreak of the Covid-19 pandemic set back this trend, and affected mostly the Operating Risk, 
as well as the Market Risk. According to the recent data of the Hellenic Association of Insurance 
Companies (H.A.I.C.), the total gross written premiums of fiscal year 2020 decreased by 3.7%, 
comparing to 2019. Regarding the Operating Cost, the insurance undertakings handled the 
challenges that were related to the continuation of operations successfully, and the Market Risk, 
which is related to the investment portfolio, after a period of prolonged volatility, presents signs of 
stabilization.  
 
The Management of the Group monitors systematically the developments on the impact of Covid-
19 pandemic on the function and financial statement of the Group.  Regarding the Annual Financial 
Statements of the Group, the major impact of Covid-19 pandemic appeared on the acquisition of 
new projects and the difficulty for the recruitment of new customers and co-operations. This 
difficulty was present mostly during the months that the government applied the restrictive 
circulation measures. It is worth noting that, despite the restrictive measures and the stagnation in 
the market, the Company, since the beginning of the pandemic provided constant support to its 
insurance agents for the achievement of its objectives, providing new and innovative digital tools 
for sale, with simultaneous training on these. As a result of the above measures, unlike the image 
of the Greek insurance market, the Group’s turnover would increase by 5.1% comparing to the 
previous year. 
 
Another significant impact of the Covid-19 pandemic was in the fiscal money markets and in general 
the investment environment. More specifically, in 2020, the Group recorded € 4,491 thous., the 
total investment income (including profit/loss from the sale and valuation of financial instruments) 
vs. € 12,698 thous. In the respective period of 2020. It is worth noting that 2019, was for the Group 
a very satisfactory fiscal year for the investment income, which exceeded the expected of the 
Budget Report. It is worth noting that the performance of the market from the impact of Covid-19 
is outside the sphere of influence of the Group. At the date of preparation of the Financial 
Statements, the investment environment presents a few signs of improvement.  
 
Moreover, as it was reasonable, the restrictive circulation measures, as applied by the government, 
decreased the overall circulation of vehicles, and as a result the number of accidents. In 2020, the 
overall cost of insurance operations (paid claims and variance of insurance provisions) amounted 
to € 115,467 thous., presenting decrease equal to € 2.415 thous. (2.0%) comparing to the previous 
period. 
Moreover, the Management of the Group organized immediately a team for crisis management, 
examining on a constant basis the liquidity ratio and the impact of Covid-19 in the financial figures 
of the Group. For the best possible preparation, the Company conducts sensitivity analyses on 
regular intervals and stress tests on the financial figures of the Group, even in extreme scenarios. 
In all aforementioned scenarios, the Solvency Ratios and the liquidity ratio remain at high levels and 
the Management of the Group has planned a series of preventive actions for the management of 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
41 
 
possible risks. 
Management monitors systematically the capital adequacy of the Company, according to Solvency 
II Directive, and proceeds to the necessary actions for the maintaining of a strong capital base and 
high quality of the investment portfolio. It is noted that on 31/12/2020, the Solvency Ratio 
strengthened by 10.6 percentage points and amounted to 174.6%. 
Moreover, European Reliance developed and implemented a series of actions, in order to ensure 
the safety and health of its people and the continuous provision of services to its insured. Many 
actions were related to the limitation of the spread of Covid-19 pandemic and contributed to the 
support of the society and mostly the human resources and the insured of the Company. 
More specifically, the actions of the Company, referring to the employees, are as follows:  
• Over 90% of the employees in teleworking status.  
• Provision of an extraordinary remuneration to the Board of Directors equal to € 545 thous.  
• Zero changes in the labor relations - Zero suspension in the employment contracts. 
• Protection of the vulnerable social groups.  
• Ordinary briefing of employees in health and safety matters.  
• Continuous disinfections of the building facilities.  
• Empowering of employees for the management of the new conditions through special 
webinars.  
• Provision of modern security tools via teleworking.  
• Digital meetings for the strengthening of the team spirit of the employees. 
The actions related to customer, insurance agents and suppliers of the Company are presented 
below: 
• Direct payment of claims equal to € 107,702 thous.  
• Provision of thirty additional credit days to the Policyholders in order to pay the 
corresponding insurance premiums. (does not apply for the Motor Sector). 
• Creation of On-line P.O. Box, for the safe receipt and dispatch of documents for the 
insurance agents and customers. 
• No overdue debt to the State, Suppliers or Insurance Agents.  
• Continuous support of the Insurance Agents for the achievement of their objectives, 
providing sufficient digital tools. 
• Continuous function of the total services and customer service. 
• Consistency in all transactions with all parties involved. 
• Suspension of scheduled trips and meetings for the safety of the insurance agents and 
customers of the Company. 
Furthermore, the following actions took place for the active support of the Society and the State: 
• Donation of 10 high-tech ventilators and other healthcare equipment to the Ministry of 
Health.  
• Provision of a free three-month insurance coverage for 250 vehicles of the Mobile Health 
Units provided by Avis to the Hellenic National Public Health Organization. 
• Participation in the scheme “Special Purpose Mobile Health Units” of the National 
Organization for Public Health.  
• Total compliance with the measures of the government.  
• Active CSR with 54 actions in 2020.

Overview of the Financial Figures  
Summary of the Key Consolidated Financial Figures 
 
The financial figures of our Group, which are mainly determined by the figures of the Parent 
Company amounted for the period 01/01/2020 to 31/12/2020 as follows: 
 
The Group’s total income (written premiums and related income, plus income from non-insurance 
activity) amounted for the above period to € 236,752 thous., versus € 225,314 thous., in the 
respective period of 2019, presenting 5.1% increase. The increase of the total income is mainly due 
to the growth of the Gross Written Premiums of the Group by 5.1%, which amounted in 2020 to € 
223,324 thous., vs. € 212,393 thous. in 2019. The largest increase was observed in the Life Insurance 
Sector (+13,5% or + € 8,632 thous.), following the Other Non-Life Sectors with increase (+2.0% or + 
859 thous.) and the Motor Sector with increase (+1.4% or + 1,440 thous.). Respectively, the income 
from subsidiaries strengthened the Group’s total income, presenting 3.9% increase (€ 507 thous.), 
comparing to the respective period, as a result of the growth of the subsidiary Alter Ego Facilities 
Management S.A., that developed new co-operations. 
 
The consolidated pre-tax profit of the Group amounted to € 24,762 thous. in the respective period 
and to € 18,526 thous. after tax, versus € 22,397 thous. pre-tax and € 17,596 thous. after tax, in the 
respective period of 2019.  
 
This formation of the profit is mainly due to the following events: 
• Increase of the earned premiums and related income by € 12,411 thous. (+6.0%),  
• increase of the paid claims of the insured by € 5,516 thous. (+5.4%),  
• Decrease of the insurance provisions, due to the payment of claims, by € 7,931 thous. 
(-50.5%) and 
• Increase of the total operating expenses (provision, administrative function and other) by € 
2,066 thous. (+6.9%) 
 
The profit of the Parent Company in 2020 amounted to € 24,370 thous. pre-tax and € 18,247 thous. 
after-tax, versus € 22,450 thous. pre-tax and € 17,694 thous. after tax in 2019. 
 
The Insurance Claims Paid presented 5.4% increase and amounted to € 107.702 thous., versus € 
102.186 thous. in 2019. More specifically, the Claims per Insurance Sector of the Group amounted 
as follows: The Life Claims presented 5.3% increase, Motor Claims presented 6.8% increase and the 
Other Non-Life Claims presented 5.1% decrease.  
 
The Investment Income of the Group amounted in fiscal year 2020 to € 3,482 thous., versus € 3,863 
thous. in 2019, presenting 9.9% decrease mainly due to the decrease of income from securities’ 
interests, which decreased from € 3,040 thous. in 2019, to € 2,672 thous. in 2020 (12.1% decrease), 
as a result of the turmoil in the international money markets, uncertain investment climate and the 
decrease of the interest rates. 
 
The total Insurance Provisions of the Group amounted to € 312,888 thous., vs. € 302,559 thous. in 
2019, presenting 3.4% increase or € 10,329 thous., a fact that shows the strengthening of the

reserves of the Company.  
 
Furthermore, the Total Operating Expenses of the Group (Remuneration and personnel expenses,

Technical Results- Gross Result of the Insurance Operations 
The technical result of the Group as a total amounted to € 46,777 thous, versus € 42,915 thous. of 
the corresponding time period of 2019, presenting 9.0% increase.  
 
The Technical Result of the Group in Life Insurance amounted to € 5,604 thous., versus € 14,640 
thous., in 2019, presenting decrease equal to 61.7%, comparing to the results of the previous year. 
The decrease in the technical result of Life insurance, is mainly due to the increase of the paid claims 
by € 2,085 thous., and the increase of the insurance provisions by € 8,188 thous., and the decrease 
of the net investment income by € 6,457 thous., as a result of the Covid-19 pandemic in the 
international money markets. 
 
The technical result of the Motor Sector amounted to € 30,619 thous., vs. € 17,919 thous., in the 
respective period of the previous year, presenting 70.9% increase, mainly due to the increase of the 
earned premiums, by € 3,242 thous., and the decrease of the insurance provisions by € 16,408 
thous., due to the payment of claims in 2020 (€ 3,809 thous., or 6.8%). 
 
The Technical Result of the Group in Other Non-Life Sector amounted to € 10,554 thous. versus € 
10,356 thous. In 2019, presenting 1.9% Increase, mainly due to the increase of the earned 
premiums by € 882 thous. and the decrease of the paid claims by € 378 thous.  
 
Profit after-tax 
The after-tax profit of the Group amounted to € 18,526 thous., vs. € 17,506 thous. In the respective 
period of 2019, presenting 5.8% increase. The result of the current year amounts, after the 
calculation of the income tax and the deferred taxation to € 6,236 thous.  
 
Earnings Per Share 
The earnings per share of the Group amounted to € 0.6860, versus € 0.6372 in the corresponding 
period of 2019.

registered directly in the Equity, since the valuation of Financial Assets equal to € 2,992 thous., and 
the Purchase of Own Shares equal to € 2,522 thous. 
 
The respective amounts of the Total Equity for the parent company amount to € 153,982 thous., 
versus € 142,112 thous. on 31/12/2019, presenting 8.4% increase. 
 
Total Assets

The cash flows from investment activities amounted to € -3,931 thous., versus € -2,633 thous. in 
the previous year. This decrease derived mostly by the increase of the purchases of intangible assets 
(software) by € 1,432 thous. 
The cash and cash equivalents of the Group in the end of the fiscal period amounted to € 18,049 
thous., versus € 16,266 thous. on 31/12/2019, presenting increase of 11.0%.  
Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) 
The Group and the Company use Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) within the framework of 
decision-making procedures on the financial, operational and strategic planning, as well as for the 
evaluation and publication of the Company’s performance. These Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) 
are used for the best possible understanding of the Company's financial and operating results, its 
financial position and statement of cash flows. The Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) must be taken 
into consideration in combination with the financial results that have been prepared in accordance 
with the IFRS, and under no circumstances may KPIs replace the results. 
 
The Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) used by the Company are described below: 
• Days Sale Outstanding DSO (in days): The DSO presents the ratio between premium debtors 
and the income from the premiums of the company and depicts in days the deadline for the 
collection of premiums from its customers, at the closing date of the balance sheet.  
• Net Profit Margin: The Ratio defines the profit from operating activities, that is the net profit 
remaining to the Company after the deduction of the cost of services and other expenses 
from the net sales (including other income). 
• Paid Claims/ Earned (gross) premiums and other related Income: This Ratio reflects the total 
claims to the insured that burden the Company as a percentage of the premiums of the 
fiscal year. 
• Acquisition Cost of new insurance premiums: This ratio reflects the total expenses paid for 
commissions and other direct written premiums expenses to the total written premiums.  

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
47 
 
• Premium Debtors Rate / Earned (gross) premiums and other related Income: This ratio 
reflects the insurance receivables on the total written premiums. A low rate reflects the 
ability of the Company to immediately collect insurance receivables. 
• Insurance Provisions Rate / Earned (gross) premiums and other related Income: This ratio 
reflects the total insurance provisions/ reserves (liabilities from insurance policies) to the 
total written premiums. A high ratio presents a good formation of the Company’s reserves. 
• Return on Equity (pre-tax): This ratio expresses how efficiently a company uses its funds to 
generate additional income and is expressed in percentage points. It is used as a ratio for 
the effectiveness of the company, presenting how much profit the Company can produce 
using the available resources invested by its shareholders (share capital) and its reserves. 
This rate represents the percentage of pre-tax profit to the total Equity in the opening 
balance sheet.  
• Return on Assets (pre-tax): This ratio reflects the return of the total assets of a company and 
allows the assessment of the effectiveness of its operations. It expresses the percentage of 
pre-tax profit to the total Assets in the beginning of the period.

Investment Analysis 
Market performances -Financial Overview 
In the first quarter of the year, financial markets presented high volatility. The reason was 
the increased uncertainty that was caused in the global economy after the lock-down, after 
the rapid spread of the Covid-19 pandemic in Europe, U.S.A., etc.) In order to decrease the 
unfortunate financial consequences, the central banks offered unlimited liquidity to the 
fiscal system, and at the same time the government decided to apply an extremely loose 
fiscal policy. 
 
The two most significant events that determined the course of the markets in the second 
semester of the previous year, was the election victory of Joe Biden, in the U.S.A., and the 
announcements of medical companies (Pfizer, Moderna, etc.) that their vaccines could 
effectively treat Covid-19. These developments caused tremendous optimist in the money 
markets and increased the assets that were positively related to the global economy (i.e. 
oil, cyclical sectors in corporate bonds and shares, etc.).   
 
At the same time, the central banks continued to provide unprecedented liquidity to the 
fiscal system, so that the economies could recess. Indicatively, the European Central Bank 
announced a purchase programme for assets of € 1.85 tril., the Pandemic Emergency 
Purchase Programme (PEPP), to handle the impact of the Covid-19 pandemic in the 
european economy. For the Greek economy, this announcement was exceptionally 
important, because the European Central Bank (through the Bank of Greece) may purchase 
Greek government bonds.  
 
Investment Portfolio  
 
The investment portfolio amounts to € 412.5 mil. in the end of 2020, versus € 382.1 mil. in 
the end of 2019. Regarding the structure of the own investment categories, in relation to 
the end of the previous year, the most important changes appear in the following 
categories: (i) corporate bonds, the rate of which increased by 2.9 p.p., and (ii) government 
bonds, the rate of which decreased by 2.8 p.p.

Description of Major Risks 
Risk Management Framework 
In European Reliance General Insurance Group of Companies, we believe that an effective 
framework for risk management is the key factor for the minimization of the Group’s risk 
exposure and the protection of the shareholders and the insured. Therefore, the Group has 
adopted risk management practices and methodologies, taking into consideration all 
related directives and requirements of the Supervisory Authority - the Department of 
Private Insurance Supervision of the Bank of Greece and the Solvency II legislation. The 
Board of Directors has established Committees, such as the Risk Management Committee 
and the and Asset, Investment & Liability Management Committee (“ALCO”). The executive 
function of Risk Management has been assigned to the Department of Risk Management.  
 
Risk Management Strategy 
European Reliance identifies the risks in its business operations and the need for effective 
risk management and has developed a special Risk Management Strategy. This Strategy 
reflects the Company's position and sets the general guidelines for risk management. More 
specifically, the Company: 
 
• 
Identifies and determines the underwriting risks, 
• 
Defines the pursued objectives and the basic guidelines of the risk management 
strategy, 
• 
Defines the risk bearing capacity, the risk appetite/tolerance and the risk profile, 
• 
Sets the ratios and the limits on the basis of which the aforementioned are specified 
and measured, 
• 
Determines the framework of governance for the Company’s Risk Management. 
 
The general objectives of the Risk Management Strategy of the Company are the following: 
 
• The improvement of the risk– reward ratio, 
• The preparation of the Company for handling risks under the risk management 
standards according to Solvency II Guidelines, 
• The implementation of the business strategy according to actions that focus on the 
audit of risks and aim to stabilize the profitability and the protection from non-
acceptable losses, for the benefit of the shareholders and the insured, 
• The assurance of the compliance with the supervisory requirements, as well as the 
best practices, 
• assurance of the effectiveness and decrease of the operating cost for risk 
management, through the limitation of the operating overlaps and avoidance of 
inappropriate or obsolete policies, procedures, methodologies, samples, audits and 
systems in line with the principle of proportionality, 
• The promotion of the risk management mentality at all Company levels. 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
54 
 
 
The Risk Management System includes procedures for the identification, measurement, 
monitoring, audit and reporting of risks. 
 
The major risks and uncertainties that could have an impact on the Group are analyzed 
below. 
1. Credit Risk 
The current or future risk for profit and funds arising from the inability of the party liable 
to meet the requirements of any contract or any other obligation that was agreed with 
Group. The term liable includes the counterparties, such as the Insurance Agents and/or 
the Insurance Brokers and Reinsurers, the Issuers of Securities, and any other Debtor and 
Guarantor. 
 
Within this framework, the following separation of risks apply: 
 
Credit risk (Counterparty Default Risk related to risk underwriting and reinsurance): The 
current or future risk for profit and funds arising from the inability of a counterparty of an 
insurance or reinsurance company to fulfill the contractual obligations. The term 
counterparty includes the contributing parties (Policyholders) of the insurance policies, as 
well as the Insurance Intermediaries and Reinsurers. 
 
Credit risk (Counterparty's Default Risk related to investments or other financial 
transactions): The current or future risk for profit and funds arising from the default of an 
Issuer of securities or of a counterparty in derivatives and/or other credit exposures. 
 
The maximum credit risk in which the Group and the Company are mostly exposed at the 
date of preparation of the financial statements report is the book value of the financial 
assets. The receivables from customers is a figure that may possible have a negative effect 
on the liquidity ratio of the Group and the Company. However, due to the increased 
number and the dispersion of customers, the credit risk rate is not high regarding these 
receivables.  
 
For this category, the Group and the Company estimate the credit risk according to the 
established policies and procedures and have proceeded to the appropriate provisions for 
the impairment. The credit risk exposure from insurance premiums on 31/12/2020 
amounts to € 16,168 thous., with marginal increase, comparing to the previous year (€ 
16,006 thous. on 31/12/2019).  
Moreover, the reinsurance receivables amounted to €11,211 thous., versus € 12,990 thous. 
On 31/12/2019, decreased by 13.7%. All of the Group’s Reinsurers have very high credit 
ratings and therefore the risk of non-paying their obligations is very low.
