The Group and the Company apply the specific credit policy which is based on the audit of 
the credit rating of customers and the effort for the effective management of the 
receivables before they become overdue, and while they are delayed or doubtful. For the 
management of the credit risk, the customers are arranged into groups depending on their 
category, their credit characteristics, their receivables and the existence of any possible 
difficulties in collectability. The receivables are considered doubtful, are re-estimated at 
the date of preparation of the financial statements and a impairment provision is formed 
for the loss that is estimated to arise from the receivables.  
 
The cash and cash equivalents are considered assets with high credit risk, since the current 
macroeconomic environment applies significant pressure to Banks. The Group and the 
Company special guidelines for the management of cash, and the possibility for risk 
exposure from transactions in foreign countries or from a counterparty is closely 
monitored.  
 
The financial assets classified as available for sale and as assets for trading purposes refer 
to investments in mutual funds and other securities. It is estimated that these two 
categories do not expose the Group and the Company to great credit risk. 
 
The loans include amounts granted to the Insurance Agents, which will be repaid through 
the retention of installments from their commissions, or they will be offset with their 
commissions. It is estimated that the aforementioned loans do not expose the Group and 
the Company to great credit risk. 
2. Liquidity and Cash Flows Risk 
Liquidity risk is the risk that the Group or the Company may not be able to fulfill its financial 
obligations on time. Liquidity Risk is kept at low levels, by retaining sufficient cash 
equivalents and credit limits that ensure the Company's ability to meet its financial 
obligations. The cash equivalents of the Group on 31/12/2020 amount to € 18,049 thous., 
versus € 16,266 thous. On 31/12/2019 (11% increase). In addition, the Group has access to 
sufficient short-term funds (although it has not received loans over the past eighteen years) 
for the coverage of extraordinary monetary needs, if this is required. Furthermore, it is 
worth noting that the Management Investment and Asset and Liability Committee (ALCO) 
monitors the liquidity risk systematically for the appropriate tools. 
3. Market/Investment Risk 
The current or future risk for profit and funds arising from fluctuations of the level and/or 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
56 
 
fluctuation of the market prices of assets and liabilities. This risk contains the following risks: 
 
Interest Rate Risk 
The current or future risk for profit and funds arising from a change in the direction, 
fluctuation or correlation of the interest rates, the shape of the yield curve, and the 
variance between the various interest rates that affect assets and liabilities. 
 
Foreign Exchange Risk 
The current or future risk for profit and funds arising from the change in the direction, 
fluctuation or correlation of foreign exchange rates that affect assets and liabilities. The 
Company’s transactions are performed in Euro and therefore the Foreign Exchange Risk is 
limited. 
 
Equity Risk 
The current or future risk for profit and funds arising from the change in the direction, 
fluctuation or correlation of the prices of shares/ratios that affect the Company’s assets. 
 
Property Risk 
The current or future risk for profit and funds arising from a change in the direction, 
fluctuation or correlation of the prices of real estate, therefore affecting the assets and the 
investments related to land, buildings and rights in rem on properties, as well as real estate 
for own use. 
 
Spread Risk 
The current or future risk for profit and funds arising from a change in the direction, 
fluctuation or correlation of the spreads over the risk free curve that affect assets and 
liabilities. This applies especially for corporate and subordinated bonds. 
4. Assets – Liabilities risk  
The current or future risk for profit and funds arising from the structure of assets and 
liabilities. The inconsistency may pertain to the maturity of the assets, interest rates, 
repricing frequency, currencies, levels and timing inconsistency of cash flows, as well as the 
maturity of the average modified duration. The Group is exposed to cash flows risk, which 
may arise from a possible future change of the fluctuated interest rates, which will 
differentiate positively or negatively the cash inflows and/or outflows related to the 
Group’s assets and/or liabilities. The Company is exposed to financial risks that refer to 
changes in the prices of securities.  
The investment revenues are shaped by factors connected to the growth of the Greek 
economy’s figures and the growth of international capital markets. For the decrease of the 
exposure to the assets-liabilities risk, the Group continues to own the larger part of the 
Investment Portfolio (over 65%) in products subject to low price fluctuation, such as 
government bonds and time deposits. Moreover, about 4.2% of the portfolio is invested in 
real estate. 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
57 
 
5. Concentration Risk 
The current or future risk for profit and funds arising from excessive overall exposure to a 
counterparty or group of counterparties, as well as to shared or interlinked factors, 
including industrial sectors or geographical regions. Concentration risk may arise in all risk 
types, such as underwriting, reinsurance, credit, market, counterparty, liquidity and 
operational risks. This risk refers to excessive exposure to individual or related groups of 
insurers and reinsurers. 
6. Operational Risk 
The current or future risk for profit and funds arising by inappropriate or inadequate 
internal procedures or by inappropriate or inadequate personnel or inappropriate or 
inadequate operating systems or from external events. 
To mitigate this risk, the Company has established adequate control and reporting 
mechanisms to identify, assess and manage the operational risk. The mechanisms that 
include the recording of internal procedures and auditing mechanisms, separation of 
competences, continuous training, procedures for approvals and agreements, 
implementation of the 4-eyes principle in all significant functions and procedures, as well 
as the supervision by the Departments of Internal Audit and Compliance. In cases where 
the consequences of the operational risk exceed the established limits, the Company has 
developed a plan to mitigate the operational risk to the acceptable limits.  
7. Business interruption risk 
The risk for the efficient service of the Company’s insured /insurance agents which can be 
caused by a business interruption. 
8. Risk of inaccuracies in data/information 
The risk of the Company in all its operations due to inaccuracies in the data and information 
used. 
9. Country Risk 
The current or future risk for profit and funds arising from some events in a specific country, 
for which the government is responsible, at least to some extent, but which certainly were 
not defined by any private company or natural person. Such possible events include the 
deterioration of economic conditions, political and social unrest, nationalization and 
expropriation of assets and the disruptive depreciation of the currency. This definition 
includes all forms of cross-border lending in one country which either involves lending to 
the government or to a bank or private business or one person. 
10. Government Bond Risk 
The current or future risk for profit and funds arising from unforeseeable changes in the 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
58 
 
value of Government Bond (EEA member countries' bonds) owned by the Company. 
11. Political Risk  
The current or future risk for profit and funds arising from political decisions that affect the 
Company's operation (i.e. changes in the tax law, depreciation of currency, lending 
possibilities, changes of the country's debt, etc.). 
12. Compliance and Legal Risk 
The current or future risk for the Company’s profit and the funds arising from violations or 
non-compliance with laws, rules, regulations, agreements, provided practices required or 
rules of conduct. 
13. Strategic Risk 
The current or future risk for profit and funds arising from adverse business decisions (i.e. 
acquisition, merger, development of new products, outsourcing, etc.), incorrect 
implementation of decisions, lack of response to the imperative changes of the market, etc. 
Strategic Risk depends on the following elements: the strategic goals of the company, the 
developed business strategies, the resources provided for the achievement of these goals 
and the financial status of the industry, within which the Group operates. 
14. Reputation Risk 
The current or future risk for profit and funds arising from the deterioration of the 
Company’s reputation or the formation of a negative perception on the image of the 
Company among counterparties, customers and/or supervisory authorities. 
15. Capital Risk 
The possibility that the Group and the Company do not have access to the required funds 
to cover its business activities or institutional obligations on the minimum or supervisory 
required levels of total equity. 
 
Within this framework: 
 
• The Company overlaps the Minimum Capital Requirement (MCR) under which the 
insurance company is led to supervisory intervention and possible revocation of 
license, 
• The Company overlaps the Solvency Capital Requirement (SCR), 
• The Company overviews the risk factors that threaten its solvency on a regular basis, 
• The Company sufficiently complies with the requirements on the quality of its 
equity (classification of Equity Tier 1, 2, 3) for the coverage of the capital 
requirements. 
 
The Company actively manages its capital structure and carries out adjustments depending 
on the economic conditions and the risk characteristics of its activities. The Company may 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
59 
 
adjust its capital structure, by readjusting the dividend for distribution to the shareholders, 
returning a fund to the shareholders or by issuing shares. There have been no changes on 
the goals, procedures and policies of the Company compared to the previous years. 
The fund management strategy implemented aims to ensure the capital adequacy of the 
Company, according to the current regulatory Solvency II framework, preventing potential 
unforeseen consequences on the capital sufficiency of the Company. The capital sufficiency 
ratios are monitored on a regular basis, in order to maximize the value for the shareholders.  
The results of the solvency ratios of the Company are available to the investors in the recent 
Solvency and Financial Condition Report (SFCR) for fiscal year 2020, in the Company’s 
website: https://www.europaikipisti.gr/en/Home 
16. Underwriting Risk 
It is the risk of at a future uncertain event that will adversely affect the insured. With the 
undersigning of the insurance policy, this risk is transferred from the insured to the Insurer. 
The Company transfers part of the underwriting risk to the reinsurer and controls its risk 
underwriting policy on a regular basis, so that the frequency and the height of the claims 
would be within the limits estimated at the pricing. The basis for the calculation of the 
underwriting risk is the Technical Provisions. Certain life insurance policies provide 
guaranteed amounts or interest rates, that are included in the terms of the policies. The 
Company performs audits in the existing products for the adequacy of the mathematical 
reserves. The complete analysis of the underwriting risk is presented in Section E of the 
present report in par. 4.2.1. 
17. Other Risks and Challenges 
The Company, within the framework of identification of the new risks and challenges, has 
included in the Risk Register, other subcategories of risks that could unfortunately affect its 
profitability and sustainability. These subcategories of risks are the result of new conditions 
that are shaped in the Greek and global insurance market. 
 
Geopolitical Risk  
 
The geopolitical risks affect the markets and the provision and ability of the consumer for 
insurance coverage and for this they are registered by the Company to the Risk Register. 
The tension in the relations U.S.A.-China, the behavior of Turkey, Brexit, social and financial 
consequences of Covid-19, the effort of the European Union to ensure the strategic 
autonomy, the update of the agenda on climate change and its geopolitical technology and 
data are between the factors that affect and will continue to affect the investment 
psychology and provision for insurance coverage of the society. 
 
Climate Change Risk  
 
The natural risks that are due to the climate change are classified in two categories: High 
risks, or risks for the appearance of certain events, and continuing risks. The increased 
natural risks are related mostly with catastrophic events, such as floods, fires, heat waves, 
etc. The continuing natural risks are related to long-term variations of the climate, such as 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
60 
 
the increase of the sea level, or the increase of the frequency, the intensity and/or the 
duration of the natural phenomena, such as floods, dry season and floods. 
 
The Company has identified the climate change risk, which may appear with frequent 
appearance of extreme natural phenomena within the framework of the in-house briefing, 
conducts scenarios in which an extreme event will affect the portfolio of the Non-Life 
Insurance Coverage (Property Sector, Motor Sector, etc.) 
 
The protection of the Company to the climate change risk, consists among others, to the 
undertaking of various risks, from various geographical locations, achieving thus 
differentiation, and limiting the possibility of causing greater damages to the underwriting 
portfolio. Thus, the Company makes extended use of the reinsurance coverage, 
transferring thus a part of the undertaken risk. 
 
A second mechanism for the treatment of the consequences of climate change is related 
to the business model of its function. Based on this, the coverage of risks that the Company 
undertakes are short-term (usually for one year) and it has the ability to re-estimate the 
risk, at least annually, taking into consideration the new data and readjusting the insurance 
premiums properly. 
 
Pandemic Risk 
 
Regarding the spread and course of the Covid-19 pandemic, the Company has examined 
the financial and health impact. More specifically, the Company examined a possible 
scenario for a new financial crisis as a result of the pandemic, if the banks, due to the lack 
of the liquidity in the market, deal with an increase in the default of payments. This could 
further tighten the credit conditions, despite the policies of the central banks and lead to a 
financial crisis, similar to 2008. 
 
The Company, within the framework of insider training, examined the possibility of variance 
of the morbidity and mortality of its portfolio, although with an Executive Committee Act, 
the hospitalization of the verified and suspect cases of Covid-19 pandemic, is mandatory in 
public hospitals, since the amount of the traditional Life insurance policies of the Company 
is low, comparing to the main volume of the Non-Life insurance policies.  
 
The Company, through the Human Resources Department, ensures the compliance of the 
personnel in Health and Safety regulations. After the outbreak of the pandemic, the rules 
were stricter and always based on the guidelines of the state mechanism, and the 
supervision of the application was constant. In that way, it is ensured that the personnel, 
insurance agents and customers, will stand in the Company’s buildings, will not be exposed 
to health-care risk and the Company will not consist the center for the outbreak of the 
pandemic.   
 
It is worth noting that the Management of the Group has activated the team for crisis 
management, examining on a constant basis the liquidity ratio and the impact of Covid19 
in the financial figures of the Group. For the best possible preparation, the Company 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
61 
 
conducts sensitivity analyses on regular intervals and stress tests on the financial figures of 
the Group, even in extreme scenarios. In all aforementioned scenarios, the Solvency Ratios 
and the liquidity ratio remain at high levels and the Management of the Group has planned 
a series of preventive actions for the management of possible risks. 
 
Telecommuting Risk  
 
Within the same framework, since the new working reality shaped new conditions and risks, 
the Company, by identifying the need for remote working, coordinated the co-operation of 
the Human Resources Division, the IT Division, the Data Governance Division and the 
Information Security Officer, to give the opportunity to over 90% of the employees for 
remote working, through 250 new encrypted laptops. Within the same framework, the 
employees were encouraged to manage the new conditions, through special webinars and 
performed a total of webinars to enhance the coherence of the employees.  
 
Cybersecurity and Data Security Risk  
The health-care dimension of the pandemic, is expected, along with other exiting factors, 
to increase the digitalization/ automation in many working positions. The Company 
examines totally the digitalization of production sectors and automation of reports. The 
Digitalization and the new technologies pertain risks. These are evaluated in the Risk 
Register in combination with the Cybersecurity and data security, that develop at the same 
time with the development of technology and new systems. The Company’s objective is to 
ensure that it will be ready to face any type of information and system risk, since it owns a 
special Department that focuses on the management and measurement of these risks.  
 
Sustainability Risk 
 
The Group, within the framework of the sustainability risk management, evaluates all 
strategic, business and organizational measures for the audit and monitoring that are used 
in the management of other risks. The concern of the Group is the existence of an excellent 
system of Corporate Governance, and this is imprinted in the Statement of Corporate 
Governance.  
 
Our Group is in total compliance with the Greek and international legislation and the 
implementations of policies of Human Rights. The company provides for the confidentiality 
of the information and meets all Principles for the Protection of Personal Data. Within the 
same framework, our Group has totally complied with the new General Data Protection 
Regulation (GDPR), the new European Insurance Distribution Directive (IDD) and the new 
Regulation (EU) No. 1286/2014 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 26th 
November 2014 regarding the key information documents for packaged retail investment 
and insurance products (PRIIP). 
 
In the field of Labour Relations and Practices, the Group European Reliance puts special 
emphasis on the adoption of policies and practices that enhance personal growth and the 
development of the employees. The Corporate Code of Conduct of European Reliance 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
62 
 
General Insurance Co S.A. creates a framework that is based on the principles of good faith, 
trading practices and professional conduct, so that all employees will be aware of the 
Company’s principles for good practice and ethics, as they have been adopted. The Internal 
Regulation of Corporate Governance and Operation contributes to the creation of a 
healthy, safe and pleasant working environment, promoting transparency in all of the 
Group’s activities.  
 
Regarding the environmental risks, the reduction of emissions, through the use of new 
sustainable technologies and audit methods equals to our active participation in the efforts 
for improvement of the environment, prevention of biodiversity loss and for protection of 
the ecosystem. European Reliance establishing proper energy management practices, 
reduces the emissions of greenhouse gases, limits the risks related to energy safety and 
establishes a framework for decrease of the energy cost for its operations. The Group 
applies a series of actions and innovations to decrease the environmental footprint with 
the active participation of the human resources. The practices applied by the Group, among 
others, is the energy upgrade of the central building, the investment in Renewable Energy 
Sources (R.E.S.), the investment in Led Lightning, the decrease in water consumption, the 
sustainable management of the central heating of buildings, the technical maintenance, for 
the saving of energy, the energetically sustainable fleet of vehicles, as well as the 
participation of the Group in the Green Angels community.  
 
In the field of Responsible and Fair Provision of Services, European Reliance, through the 
institutional bodies and its representatives, aims to improve the practices of corporate 
governance and the insurance culture with successive actions among which, the function 
of the Compliance Department. The four pillars in which Compliance is based that 
strengthen the Responsible and Fair Provision of Services is, the Safety of the financial 
system, Business Ethics, Market Integrity and Customer’s Interests. 
 
Regarding the Consumer Issues, among others, in cooperation with the seven Departments 
of the Company (Customer’s Services, Call Center, Compliance, Legal Service, Marketing, 
Project Management and CRM) we conducted an annual research on customer’s 
satisfaction. The results confirmed once more the solvency and credibility levels of the 
Company.  
 
The Social Contribution of the Group is important, since it has developed mechanisms and 
procedures, based on which we register and prioritize issues related to Corporate Social 
Responsibility, their importance for the Stakeholders and external Environment. Our 
actions as a responsible corporate citizen derive from open debates, the effort for 
communication and are subject to constant evaluation and reviews.

on the (EU) Regulation 2019/1238 of the European Council and the Council of Europe,

The Company puts special emphasis on the management of fraud, since in the modern

2021 Prospects and Growth 
The Covid-19 pandemic continues to impact the financial activities, in a global and national 
level, causing recession and uncertainty, since the return to positive growth prospect is 
intrinsically linked with the course of the pandemic. 
 
Based on the Government Budget of 2021, the GDP is expected to present 4.8% annual 
growth rate in 2021, comparing to 2020, and the largest international credit rating agencies 
perform estimates for the Greek economy, forecasting for 2021, 3.5% to 5% development. 
In an effort to recover a part of the lost dynamic, we expect the contribution of all 
components of the domestic demand, less public consumption, which is expected to 
decrease by 1.9%, vs. 2.1% increase in 2020, due to the high expenses of 2020 that are paid 
for the treatment of the Covid-19 pandemic. 
 
The negative impact of the pandemic is estimated to gradually decrease in 2021, since the 
implementation rate of the vaccination accelerates, and the limitations in transportation 
would gradually decrease, traveling and tourism will be allowed again, the Group will expect 
a growth course, in the second semester of the year, with increase of the total income. 
 
For 2021, the Management of the Group expects increase of the new business in the Life 
Sector, because since 2020, the market has been affected by the pandemic, which has 
caused feelings of uncertainty to the citizens, on health and hospital coverages. 
 
Yet, it is estimated that the lifting of the Covid-19 restrictions, the expected recovery of the 
touristic activity and the climate changes, will affect positively the new business of Non-Life 
insurance of the Group.

Transactions with Related Parties 
Related parties include companies in which the Parent Company has the control. Related 
parties are the members of the management of the Group, first-degree relatives, 
companies that are controlled by these persons, in which these members have a 
substantial influence on the management and fiscal policy. 
 
All transactions with related parties are made on the same terms that apply for similar 
transactions with non-related parties. Transactions and balances between the Group’s 
companies are eliminated at the consolidation.

Dividend for fiscal year 2020 
For the fiscal year ended on 31/12/2020, the Board of Directors proposes distribution of 
dividend from the Company’s profit of fiscal year 2020, equal to € 7,961,972.10 an amount 
that corresponds to € 0.29 per share. It is noted that the above dividend per share will be 
increased depending on the number of Own Shares owned by the Company, at the Record 
Date. The Company’s Own Shares at the date of preparation of the Financial Statements 
amounted to € 963,770. Based on the above, the dividend amounted to € 0.30 per share. 
 
Remuneration through Distribution of Profit 
For the fiscal year that ended on 31/12/2020, the Board of Directors proposes the 
distribution of dividend to the personnel from the profit of fiscal year 2020, equal to € 
805,757.73 thous., which is equal to € 742.,46.00 thous., as remuneration and € 63,211.73 
as labor contributions. 
 
Extraordinary General Meeting of Shareholders of 02/03/2021 “Cancellation of Own Shares 
and Amendment of the Articles of Association” 
On March 2, 2021 at 14:00, the Company performed via teleconference and with the use 
of the special platform of the Athens Stock Exchange, AXIA e-Shareholders Meeting, the 
Extraordinary General Meeting of Shareholders. The General Meeting of Shareholders 
decided and approved the proposal of the Board of Directors and more specifically decided 
and approved the cancellation, according to article 49 of L. 4548/2018, of nine hundred, 
ten thousand, three hundred, sixteen (910,316) own shares of the Company, the decrease 
of the share capital of the Company by the amount of five hundred, seventy-three 
thousand, four hundred ninety-nine Euro and eight cents (€ 573/499.08), the 
corresponding amendment of article 5 of the Article of Association of the Company on the 
share capital, in order to imprint the above decrease and its codification in a unified text, 
and authorized the Board of Directors of the Company in order to undertake all required 
actions and communications for the completion of the above subjects. The decision of the 
Greek General Commercial Registry will be announced at the date of preparation of the 
Financial Statements.  
 
Provision of a special license of article 101 of Law 4548/2018 
On 27/01/2021, the Company made an announcement in the Athens Stock Exchange and 
the investment website of the Company, on the decision of the Board of Directors of the 
Company for the issuance of a corporate guarantee by “European Reliance General 
Insurance Co. S.A. To the Bank of Greece S.A., in favor of the subsidiary “Alter Ego Facilities 
Management S.A.’ for the increase of the already existing lending limit pf Alter Ego S.A., 
and the decision for the granting of a special license, with duration six (6) months, for the 
preparation and undersigning of the above transaction of the Company with the Company 
with trade name “Alter Ego Facilities Management S.A.”, which is a related undertaking 
with the Company.   
 
The Board of Directors of the Company took into consideration the report of 25/01/2021

of Ms. Despoina Marinou, Certified Public Accountant (with SOEL No. 17681) of the auditing

The entire Assessment Report of the fair and reasonable of the transaction, based on Article

The Company, after analyzing the Deviations to the New Law regarding the compliance of

planned in the first semester of 20201 all necessary actions for the total compliance of the 
Company with the Law. 
 
Within this framework, the Company has already performed the following actions: 
• Amendment of the Organization Chart, 
• Preparation of a Policy for the Fit and Proper of the Board Members, which has been

• Update of the Operation of Regulation of the Risk Management Committee, in

Control System. 
• Update of the Operation of Regulation for the Audit Committee, in order to include 
the requirements of the new law, for the function and supervision of the Internal 
Audit Function. 
• Update of the Operation of Regulation of the Corporate Governance, Remuneration 
and Nomination Committee, that in European Reliance acts as a Committee, in 
order to incorporate the requirements of the Law. 
For its full compliance with the Law, the Company has scheduled up to 17/07/2021, the 
following actions: 
 
• Election of a new Board Member, taking into consideration the independence 
criteria and diversity, defined by the new Law. 
• Election of a new BoD Committees, taking into consideration the independence 
criteria of their members, as defined by the new Law. 
• Update of the Internal Regulation of Corporate Governance and Operation of the

Non-Financial Information  
European Reliance General Insurance Co. S.A, since its first establishment up to the present 
day, constantly invests on its people, the society, the environment, aiming at the 
strengthening of the sustainable development and provision of actual support. 
  
The strategy of European Reliance Group of Companies on the CSR actions is totally in 
compliance with the principles of ISO 26000 Certification, the GRI Reporting Standards, the 
UN Global Compact and the 17 Sustainable Development Goals.  
 
Since 2011, European Reliance publishes an analytical Sustainable Development Report 
aiming at the detailed briefing of all interested parties on the CSR actions and the multiple 
benefits of these actions. 
 
2020, was a very difficult year, since Covid-19 pandemic had a major impact on the society 
in a global level. European Reliance stood next to the society, its human resources and 
insured, providing practical support via many actions, especially designed to respond to the 
needs. Moreover, European Reliance supported through donations the public health 
system and adopted since the first days, all measures necessary for the limitation of the 
spread of the pandemic in the country.  
 
Moreover, setting human nature and environment in the center of attention, our Group 
supported the local entities and non-profitable organization, thus contributing to their 
precious work, enhanced sport organizations and education entities, at a time period when 
the consequences of the health crisis started to appear. Moreover, European Reliance 
continued with intense interest the action for decrease of the environmental footprint and 
performed a very special investment in Renewable Energy Resources, through the 
acquisition of the Company AEM Energy Systems, which owns a Photovoltaic Power Station.  
 
The Non-Financial Information Report, according to Law 4548/2018, that it applies, aims at 
the briefing of all interested parties of the Group, regarding the policy, schemes, actions 
and future actions of the Group, on Sustainability Issues and other significant non-Financial 
Data.

Quality of Services and Quality Assurance Systems 
 
The Management of the Group applies the Total Quality Management and pay special 
attention to the Quality Assurance Systems. European Reliance is the only Insurance 
Company in Greece with 4 International ISO standards (ISO 9001:2015, ISO 27001:2013, 
ISO 19600:2014, ISO 50001:2018). 
 
Furthermore, European Reliance is the first Insurance Company certified 
for the total of its services with ISO 9001:2000 Certification in 2002 and 
nowadays retains this certification, according to the new ISO 9001:2015 
certification. This standard ensures that the Quality Management System 
that European Reliance applies in the field of planning and provision of 
insurance services, ensures the proper compliance of the criteria for 
preserving the quality of the provided services and simultaneously the 
Company enhances its reputation, decreases the productive cost and 
improves the management of its resources. 
 
In 2017, the Company received a Certification by the Inspection and 
Certification Body “TÜV HELLAS (TÜV NORD)”, in accordance with 
international standard ISO 27001:2013 Certification, which defines the 
requirements for the Information Security Management System and 
refers to all of the Company’s procedures. This certification ensures that 
European Reliance manages and protects the valuable information assets 
and complies with the applicable Legislation and Regulations.  
 
Moreover, with the above certification, the Company proves in action 
that the shielding of the information security of customers is of significant 
importance. 
 
In 2020, European Reliance successfully completed the audit procedure 
for the alignment of the Compliance Management System of the 
Company, according to the requirements of the international standard 
ISO 19600:2014. This certification is a new distinction for the Company 
and is classified in within the framework of the strategic partnership of 
the Management of the Company on the complete, prompt and effective 
compliance, continuous regulatory changes that take place over the past 
years in the insurance sector, protecting simultaneously the interests of 
the consumers and every interested party.

2. Human Rights 
 
European Reliance Group of Companies voluntarily participates in the UN Global Compact 
aiming to apply the terms of sustainable business function and sustainable business 
development in its policies by standing for and promoting the principles that concern 
human rights, labor rights, the protection of the environment and the fight against 
corruption. 
 
The UN Global Compact encourages businesses to promote and apply the ten principles for 
the human rights, labor, environment and the fight against corruption, that are based on 
the following documents: 
• Universal Declaration of Human Rights 
• Declaration on Fundamental Principles and Rights at Work 
• Rio Declaration on Environment and Development, 
• United Nations Convention against Corruption. 
The Principles of the United Nations Global Compact on human rights and the actions of 
European Reliance, in compliance with the following principles: 
 
Principle No. 1  Businesses should support and respect the protection of internationally 
proclaimed human rights. 
Principle No.2  Businesses should make sure that they are not complicit in human rights 
abuses.

Our Group is in total compliance with the Greek and international legislation and the 
implementations of policies for Human Rights. 
For the Group, these principles are self-evident values and are adhere to all of our 
partnerships. 
 
The internal regulation of Corporate Governance and Function of the Group, and in general 
the function of the Group, is governed by the principles of justice, respect of personality, 
objectivity, meritocracy, complete abstinence from any form of discrimination and 
harassment, provision for the benefit of equal opportunities with further provisions for the 
maximum safety of the personnel, in the case of an unfortunate event. 
 
The company provides for the confidentiality of the information and meets all Principles for 
the Protection of Personal Data.  
 
Since the beginning of 2017, our Group has performed all necessary actions and has complied 
with the new General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR), the new European Insurance 
Distribution Directive (IDD) and the new Regulation (EU) No. 1286/2014 of the European 
Parliament and of the Council of 26th November 2014, regarding the key information 
documents for packaged retail investment and insurance products (PRIIP). 
 
3. Labor relationships- Labor practices 
 
Our people's education and high training levels in their working field is a major pillar for our 
Company. Their constant training and experience are the key elements that make the 
Group a leading force in the Greek insurance market.  
 
The goal of European Reliance Group of Companies is to adopt policies and practices that 
enhance the personal development and advancement of the employees. In European 
Reliance, daily work gives meaning to the life of the employee, purpose and a feeling of 
self-fulfillment, as the employee participates in a valuable project. The Corporate Code of 
Conduct of European Reliance creates a framework that is based on the principles of good 
faith, trading practices and professional conduct, so that all employees will be aware of the 
Company’s applied principles for good practices and ethics. The Internal Regulation of 
Corporate Governance and Operation contributes to the creation of a healthy, safe and 
pleasant working environment, promoting transparency in all of our Group’s activities.  
 
The Principles of the UN Global Compact on labor rights and the actions of European 
Reliance that are in in compliance with the following principles: 
Principle No. 3  Businesses should uphold the freedom of association and the effective 
recognition of the right to collective bargaining. 
Principle No. 4  Businesses should uphold the elimination of all forms of forced and 
compulsory labour. 
Principle No. 5  Business should uphold the effective abolition of child labour.

European Reliance sets the bar high on issues of labour practices and relations.

• creates working conditions that enable our employees to balance their private life

• Creates new working positions. Since 2015, our Group increased its personnel to 
194 people, 
• accepts the return of every employee that for personal reason interrupted the 
employment contract for a certain time period (100% of the employees requesting 
to return to their working position was accepted), 
• Ensures the health and safety of the employees in the working spaces and complies 
with all relevant regulations (in 2020 there has not been a single injury recorded), 
• Applies fair remuneration policies, according to the productivity and the 
professional experience of each employee, 
• Provides 100% insurance coverage for all employees that have been working in the 
company for more than one year, like the Group Health Insurance Coverage, 
• Actively supports woman’s right to pregnancy,

the executive directors are female),

the company and urges them to undertake mentoring and coaching roles to the 
new employees,

employees’ common interests, 
• Supports life-long learning programs for the employees with seminars and trips in 
Greece and abroad, aiming at their best training and therefore ultimate customer 
service experiences, 
• Maintains a clear attitude on "money laundering" and has established procedures 
to prevent cases of money laundering (every financial /administrative officer 
involved in issues of compliance and internal audit has attended relevant seminars), 
• Rewards employees for their social work and initiatives for Corporate Social 
Responsibility.

4. Environment 
 
European Reliance, as a responsible corporate citizen, perceives the concept of sustainable 
development as an attempt to create a competitive economy of minor pollutants that uses 
its resources effectively and takes into account the protection of the environment.  
 
The use of new sustainable technologies and audit methods that contribute to the 
reduction of emissions, is our active participation in the efforts for improvement of the 
environment, the prevention of biodiversity loss and for ecosystem protection. 
 
Moreover, climate change has been recognized by the European Council and other 
member states as an emergency situation.  The European Council concluded that climate 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
85 
 
change is an “existential threat:”. Within this framework, the E.U. Committed to ensure a 
climate neutral by 2050 and achieve an even more ambitious target for the decrease of the 
emissions, at least by 55% by 2030, comparing to 1990. Moreover, E.U. Records totally 
increased financial losses, which are mainly due to the frequent appearance of extreme 
weather conditions, as a result of the climate.  These losses, exceed the average amount of 
€ 12 bil. annually. 
 
More specifically, European Reliance Group of Companies embraces the idea of sustainable 
development, as defined in the Declaration of the United Nations in Africa in September 
2002: “Johannesburg Declaration on Sustainable Development” and as developed in Rio de 
Janeiro in June 1992: “Rio Declaration on Environment and Development”. The initiative 
for new patterns of corporate behavior is part of our strategy, not a communications plan.  
 
Moreover, in 2020, the Company organized with great success and received the review of 
ISO 50001:201 Certification by the Inspection and Certification Body “TÜV HELLAS (TÜV 
NORD)”. It is noted that European Reliance is the only Insurance Company that has received 
this certification. 
European Reliance establishing proper energy management practices, reduces the 
emissions of greenhouse gases, limits the risks related to energy safety and establishes a 
framework for decrease of the energy cost for its operations. 
 
This certificate refers to the energy management system, according to which companies 
are encouraged to act in a sustainable way and save resources. Through this certification, 
companies contribute to the protection of the environment and the climate and at the 
same time benefit from energy conservation. 
 
The Group applies a series of actions and innovations to decrease the environmental 
footprint with the active participation of the human resources. The practices applied by the 
Group are analyzed below and proved the environmental awareness of the Group.  
 
Energy upgrade of the Own-held Buildings 
 
The decrease of the carbon footprint, within the framework of our efforts to improve our 
energy profile, has been a real challenge for European Reliance. However, our 2011 
investment on the Energy Management and Monitoring Information System for the 
decrease of the energy consumption per employee has proven to be a very strategic step. 
More specifically, we have created a personal energy profile for every employee and for 
every device in our central offices and we have determined our average energy needs. 
Every energy consuming device has been time programmed to shut down automatically, 
after the termination of the work shift of the employees. 
 
Since 2011, the effort increased our environmental awareness and reduced the carbon 
footprint of every working position in the central offices of European Reliance General 
Insurance Co. S.A. In 2020, the Group continued the Plan for Energy Upgrade of the 
Buildings in the Headquarters of Chalandri, whereas in 2021, the Group has budgeted 
projects for the energy upgrade in Thessaloniki and Patra. 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
86 
 
 
 
Investment in Renewable Energy Sources (R.E.S.) 
Our Group, faithful to its principles, to support the use and development of the Renewable 
Energy Sources as a mean for sustainable development in October 2012 undersigned a 
contract with the Public Power Corporation (Operator of Electricity Market) to sell the 
electric power produced by the Group’s investment in a fixed equipment of 130 
photovoltaic power stations. The actions of the Group, for the creation of a photovoltaic 
power station on the rooftop of our central building- Europlaza-, is part of our actions for 
the decrease of the carbon footprint, through the minimization of pollutant power sources. 
According to the Operator of the Electricity Market, the total electricity production of the 
R.E.S. of our Group in 2020 amounted to 45,355 kWh. 
 
Furthermore, the Group European Reliance, in February 2020, purchased 100% of the 
share capital of the Company AEM Energy Systems Private Company, active in the sector of 
energy production through the use of the photovoltaic station in the region of Thessaly, of 
nominal power 0.5 MWh, which is related to the medium voltage network of the Public 
Power Corporation. This strategic investment of the Group proves that the maintenance of 
the natural resources, through the use of the renewable energy resources, is part of the 
Group’s philosophy. 
 
Led Lighting Investment 
Within the framework of using Renewable Energy Sources, our Group has committed to 
replace all traditional lamps in our Central Offices (274 Kifisias Avenue, Chalandri) with 
energy efficient LED lamps of the new generation. The capital required for the completion 
of such an investment proves the Company’s liquidity ratio and sustainable development, 
and its ability to guarantee amortization of a big investment. 
 
The energy modernization project in our Group’s central offices began in 2012, with the 
replacement of lamps in two floors, and was completed by the end of 2013 with the total 
replacement of all lamps (including the outdoor projector lamps). It is estimated that we 
achieved decrease of the produced carbon dioxide equal to 31 tones/ per year in the 
atmosphere (according to the GreenHouse Gas Protocol calculation method, where 1 MWh 
of electrical power consumption produces 0,812 tn CO2) and the saving of energy resources 
amounts to 54%. 
 
The significance of this project is due to the saving of valuable resources, the decrease of 
the local temperature in the building’s central offices, the formation of a better working 
environment due to improved luminance and the fact that this change directly affected the 
Group’s energy profile, without hindering the working methods of the employees. Since 
this change had benefited the internal and outdoor spaces of the Organization, it was 
positively accepted by all employees. 
 
Water Resources Conservation 
Within the Sustainability framework, all socially responsible companies, try to reduce the 
environmental footprint, with respect to the vulnerable social groups, try to reduce the 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
87 
 
environmental footprint, aim at the conservation of water resources and the sensitization 
of all interesting parties. European Reliance, since the first Sustainability Report (2011), 
actively participates in the Millennium Development Goals (Water Scarcity Issue). 
 
European Reliance has invested in environmentally-friendly hydraulic facilities and in water 
supply systems with sensor technology for reduced daily water consumption.  
 
European Reliance, a proud member of Green Angels Community  
 
Since 2015, European Reliance is an active member of the first Greek Society of 
environmentally responsible businesses, under the discrete title “Green Angels”. The Green 
Angels society operates under the auspices of the Ministry of Environment and Energy and 
the Athens Stock Exchange. The goal of this initiative is to reduce the carbon footprint of 
Greece through constant improvement of the environmental performance and the 
protection of Greek forests. 
 
Central heating of the Headquarters 
 
Natural gas reduces energy consumption and the carbon footprint of European Reliance 
General Insurance Co. S. A. Among all conventional fuels, natural gas emits fewer 
pollutants. For this reason, the use contributes to the decrease of the greenhouse and acid 
rain effect. the average fuel consumption and the atmospheric pollution. European 
Reliance, aiming to create an environmentally friendly profile, has renovated the heat 
generators of the central building and has invested in modern building insulation materials 
for the best possible cooling with minimum electric power consumption. In 2015, our 
Group shielded all of our building facilities, to cover our needs with the least possible 
energy resources. In all our efforts for improvement of the energy profile, we have been 
supported by our subsidiary Alter Ego Facilities Management and its specialized services 
(Sustainable Management Solutions). 
 
Technical Maintenance of the Headquarters 
 
The technical maintenance of the buildings’ facilities and the constant audits applied by our 
technicians aim at the best possible function of the facilities (highest profit with less 
resources). The improvement of our regulations, the immediate record of faulty points and 
the prompt intervention for their correction, contribute to energy conservation without 
obstructing the daily rhythm of the company.  
Moreover, the subsidiary company, Alter Ego, uses high quality and environmentally 
friendly cleaning materials certified by ISO:9001, ISO:14001 & OHSAS. 
The Ecolabel products are certified and have their own registration date and number. 
 
Fleet of Corporate Vehicles 
 
The Group owns a fleet of long-term leased vehicles and has selected new technology 
vehicles, according to the E.U requirements, with low carbon dioxide emissions, low energy 
consumption and engines with Euro 5 specifications, that can daily circulate in the center 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
88 
 
of Athens. Since 2021, the Group is committed for new vehicles with Euro 6 engines, such 
as plug-in hybrid electric vehicles (PHEV). 
 
5. Responsible provision of equitable services 
As a responsible corporate citizen, European Reliance aims through its institutional bodies 
and representatives, to improve the corporate governance practices and the insurance 
sector’s culture. The Group cooperates with all institutional bodies (Hellenic Management 
Association, Hellenic Association of Insurance Companies, Direct Payment System) and 
accepted the Solvency II legislation and the requirements of the Supervisory Authority 
(Bank of Greece) being fully aware of the responsibilities. 
The Group’s Management, in order to ensure the responsible and fair provision of services 
has performed a series of successive actions, including the operation of the Compliance 
Department. 
 
The Compliance Department provides for the adjustment and continuous function of the 
insurance company for the current applicable legislation and regulatory decisions. 
Compliance is applied in all sectors of European Reliance as a standard of corporate culture, 
enhancing our corporate identity, as it is a part of our philosophy and a commitment of the 
Company’s Management. 
 
Our Group’s Compliance is based on the 4 following pillars that contribute to our 
Company’s responsible and fair provision of services: 
• Safety of the fiscal system 
• Business Ethics 
• Market Integrity 
• Customers’ Interest. 
Moreover, our Group pays great attention to the received Complaints, and apart from the 
Policies and procedures established for their most effective management, has additionally 
established a Complaints Committee. The Complaints Committee meets for the collection 
of information for the common causes of complaints, to draw conclusions, create and 
observe a plan of corrective actions for the limitation of the complaints and their impact 
on the quality of the company’s provided services to the customers.  
 
In European Reliance we are completely centered in our Customer’s needs. For this reason, 
European Reliance has 4 different Departments that operate with sole objective the 
customers’ service: 
• Customer Service: The Department aims at the smooth absorption of products and 
services by our customers and solves possible problems. It provides information, 
resolves problems and assures high levels of satisfaction among our customers. 
• Call Center: The Department communicates with customers in order to present and 
inform them about our Company’s products. 
• Bancassurance: The Department manages insurance products and products of 
alternative networks, according to the Company’s and the Banking Institutions’ 
policies defined by special contracts that ensure their proper compliance. 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.

• Bank Office- Analytical CRM: The Department has as objective the planning and 
implementation of our strategy, the retention and expansion of the Company’s 
clientele, through the maximum utilization of the resources of the CRM Division. 
 
6. Consumer Issues 
Customer care is part of the Group’s corporate behavior. The precise and honest update of 
our present and future customers consists one of the main priorities of European Reliance. 
Additionally, to the compliance with the legislative framework and the trading practices, 
European Reliance applies an internal code of business conduct in all stages of creation, 
promotion of product and customer service. 
Every customer is an integral part of the productive process of the Group, a fellow and a 
working partner. Our philosophy is not only expressed in our motive “It pays immediately”, 
but it is applied in our daily actions. 
 
In European Reliance the customer’s satisfaction is our first priority and the greatest 
measure of success. Therefore, the Group’s quality policy is based on the complete 
satisfaction of the customers, in all of their transactions, and in the continuous quality 
improvement of our provided insurance services, to fully cover our customers’ insurance 
needs. 
 
In 2020, according to the annual audit by the certification body TUV NORD, European 
Reliance successfully completed all the required actions and received the ISO 9001:2015 
certification. This certificate totally complies with the objectives of the Group, to retain its 
name as a synonym of high quality in the provision of insurance services and to remain one 
of the top Organizations in the insurance market.  
 
Moreover, European Reliance follows the UN guidelines for consumer protection, as 
initially formed and agreed in 1985 and as they were enhanced in 1999 with the proposals 
on Sustainable Consumption. We embrace the promotion and protection of the financial 
interests of the customer, create a framework that allows consumers to make informed 
choices, forward consumer’s information and promote the principles for sustainable 
consumption. 
 
European Reliance ensures that the promote of our products and services is performed 
with precision and with respect to the public.  
For this reason, we apply a fair marketing strategy, without false statements, term 
inconsistencies or concealment of truth.  
This obligation consists an integral part of our business ethics of our Group and a 
commitment to any of our contracting parties. 
 
In 2020, European Reliance invested in the sincerity and transparency in the 
communication with the public (in television and digital media advertisements and in 
“below the line” actions).  
Moreover, our Group fully acknowledges the enormous potential of the Internet. Through 
the incredible amount of information available on the website of our Group, social 
networks, and domains, every consumer has the opportunity to make comparisons, start 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.

90 
 
discussions and conclude to the best suitable product or service. 
 
Through this process, we aim to start an open dialogue with our current and future 
customers, improve our performances and become better listeners.  
 
In May 2020, in cooperation with seven Departments of the Company (Customer’s Services, 
Call Center, Regulatory Compliance, Legal Services, Marketing, Procedure Management 
and CRM) we conducted an annual research on customer’s satisfaction. The results did 
once again confirm the solvency and credibility levels of the Company. A few of the 
research’s findings are the following: 
• 92% of our customers remains extremely satisfied by the total of our services (89% 
in 2019) 
• 91% of our customers believes that they have an insurance plan that covers all their 
needs (88% in 2019) 
• 96% of our customers believes that the Company’s insurance agent is almost always 
available and willing to provide consulting services (94% in 2019)  
• 92% of our customers are very pleased with the claims’ procedure (88% in 2019) 
• 97% of our customers would recommend our Company and 96% would recommend 
their insurance agent to new customers (95% and 93% respectively in 2019), mainly 
due to the service and credibility. 
7. Social Responsibility 
The objective of our Group, as a responsible corporate citizen, is the expansion and 
enhancement of its social role through actions that contribute to social cohesion. Especially 
nowadays, that the citizens of Greece experience the consequences of the economic crisis, 
it is our responsibility to strengthen social institutions and promote collective actions. 
 
European Reliance, along with its main corporate activities, aims to contribute to our 
society. We set high standards for corporate social actions through the continuous 
cooperation with various local organizations, and we contribute to the improvement of 
living standards, by combating social exclusion. 
 
Thought the development of special mechanisms and procedures, our Group registers and 
prioritizes issues related to Corporate Social Responsibility, depending on their importance 
for the Interested Parties, the external Environment. Our actions as a responsible corporate 
citizen derive from open debates, the effort for communication and are subject to constant 
evaluation and reviews. 
 
Regarding the corporate social responsibility, we follow the Millennium Declaration for the 
8 Millennium Development Goals (as mentioned above in Section 2 “Human Rights”).  
The method to achieve the aforementioned goals requires certain policies and the decision 
to proceed to cooperation with a social institution depends on its values, unique 
knowledge, special abilities and the Company’s resources. One of the common 
characteristics and our goals for the future is the elimination of human suffering and its 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.

consequences.

Information in accordance with article 4, par. 7 of L. 3556/2007 in line with the provisions 
of par. 8, Article 4 of L. 3556/2007and L. 4548/2018. 
 
Pursuant to Article 4, par. 7 of L. 3556/2007, art. 50, par. 2 of L. 4548/2018 and according 
to the Company’s Articles of Association and other decisions of the General Meeting of 
Shareholders and the Board of Directors, the Company is obliged to publish in the present 
Statement of the Board of Directors detailed information on the following subjects: 
 
Own Shares 
The Company acquired Own Shares, within the framework of the approved Scheme for 
Repurchase of Own Shares by the General Meeting of Shareholders (meeting of May 21, 
2019).  
The Management of the Company received approval to purchase 2,750,367 own shares, in 
the time period from 01/07/2019 up to 30/06/2021 on prices ranging from € 2.00 
(minimum acquisition price) to € 6.00 (maximum acquisition price) per share. On 
31/12/2020, the Company holds 812,464 Own Shares equal to € 3,540,376.63. On March 
2, 2021, the Company performed an Extraordinary General Meeting of Shareholders, for 
the cancellation of Own Shares (read section “Significant events after the end of the 
reporting period”) 
 
Structure of the Company's share capital 
 
The Company's share capital amounts to € 17,327,316.51 and is divided into 27,503,677 
common nominal shares, with nominal value of 0.63 € each (Decision of the Extraordinary 
General Meeting of Shareholders on 16/12/2014). The total of the Company’s shares 
consists of common, nominal shares with voting rights and there are no special categories 
of shares. The shares of the Company are listed in the Athens Stock Exchange and are 
therefore intangible. 
 
The rights of the Company’s shareholders deriving from the share correspond to the 
percentage of the capital in which the paid-up value of the share corresponds. Each share 
includes all rights and liabilities provided by Law and the Company’s Articles of Association 
and especially:  
• The right to participate and vote in the Company's General Meeting of 
Shareholders.  
• The right on the dividend from the Company’s annual profit, which amounts to 35% 
of the net profit after the deduction of the statutory reserve and other profit 
referred to in par. 1 of article 3 of C.L. 148/67 and art. 161 of L. 4548/2018. The rest 
of the amount is distributed in total or in parts to the shareholders or it is provided 
for the formation or strengthening of any of the reserves or deductions, upon 
decision of the Ordinary General Meeting of Shareholders.  
• All the shareholders that are referred to in the shareholders’ register at the date of 
determination of the beneficiaries for dividends, are entitled to receive dividends. 
The dividend of each share is paid to the shareholder within two (2) months after 
the date of the Ordinary General Meeting of Shareholders that approved the annual 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
98 
 
fiscal statements. The method and the place of payment is announced in the Daily 
Price Bulletin and the ATHEX website. The claim for dividend is written off and the 
corresponding amount goes to the Greek State, five (5) years after the year in which 
the General Meeting of Shareholders approved the distribution.  
• The pre-emptive right in every increase of the share capital of the Company in cash 
and the subscription of new shares.  
• The right to receive a copy of the financial statements and reports of the certified 
public accountants and the Company’s Board of Directors.  
 
The General Meeting of the shareholders reserves all rights at the liquidation (according to 
Article 29 of its Article of Association).  
The liability of the Company’s shareholders is limited to the nominal value of the shares 
they own.  
 
Limitation in the transfer of the Company’s shares 
 
The shares of the Company are intangible, are traded freely in the Athens Stock Exchange 
and are transmitted as the Law applies, and according to its Article of Association, no 
limitations apply in their transmission. According to art. 4 of L. 3016/2002, as applicable, 
the independent, non-executive members of the Board of Directors of the Company may 
not, among others, hold shares equal to over 0.5% of the paid up share capital. On 
31/12/2020, no independent Board member hold any shares over 0.5% of the paid up share 
capital of the Company. 
 
According to article 19 of Regulation 596/2014 of the European Council and the Council (as 
well as the authorized Regulation 2016/522 and the Executive Regulation 2016/523 of the 
European Commission), the persons that exercise managerial duties and those closely 
related to these persons, ought to notify on the transactions performed, directly or 
indirectly on their behalf and are related with the shares or debt instruments of the 
Company or derivatives or other financial instruments related to these after the completion 
of the amount of € 5,000 (without offsetting) for one year. For all of the above liable 
persons, within 2020, the transaction of amounts (over € 5,000) was published in the 
website of the Athens Stock Exchange. 
 
Significant direct or indirect holdings within the meaning of the provisions of Art. 9 to 11 of 
L. 3556/2007. 
Significant direct or indirect holdings within the meaning of the provisions of Art. 9 to 11 of 
L. L. 3556/2007 are the following: 
 
• European Reliance General Insurance Co. S.A. (parent company) 
• Alter Ego S.A. 97.30% 
• European Reliance Asset Management M.F.M.S.A.: 99.01% 
• Reliance Single-Member Insurance Agents S.A. 100.00% 
• AEM Energy Systems Private Company 99.96% 
• Partner Hotel S.A. 48.65% (through Alter Ego S.A.)

Notes on the Financial Statements 
1. General Information on the Company and the Group 
1.1 Group’s composition 
 
EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO. S.A. (S.A. Reg. No. 12855/05/Β/86/35), 
General Electronic Commercial Registry (G.E.M.I.) No. 322801000), was established in 
1977. The Company’s objective, according to Art. 4 of its Article of Association, is to provide 
all kinds of insurance and reinsurance coverage. Its registered offices are at 274 Kifisias 
Avenue, Postal Code 15232, Chalandri. 
 
The Group includes the companies: 
• European Reliance General Insurance Co. S.A. (parent company) 
• Alter Ego S.A. 
• European Reliance Asset Management M.F.M.S.A. 
• Reliance Single-Member Insurance Agents S.A. 
• AEM Energy Systems Private Company 
• Partner Hotel S.A. 
 
1.2 Composition of the Board of Directors of the Parent Company on 31/12/2020: 
 
The Board of Directors consists of: 
 
1. Nikolaos Chalkiopoulos - Chairman (executive member) 
2. Stefanos Verzovitis - Vice-Chairman (executive member) 
3. Christos Georgakopoulos - CEO (executive member) 
4. Keith Morris - (non-executive, independent member)  
5. George Diamantopoulos - (non- executive, independent member) 
6. Eric Christopher Sharp (executive member) 
7. George Konstantinidis - (non-executive member) 
8. Christopher Poulios - (non-executive, independent member) 
9. Theodor Chronis - (non-executive member)  
 
The members’ term of office expires on 30/06/2024.  
 
1.3 Regulatory framework 
“EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE Co. S.A.” is governed by the provisions of Law 
4548/2018 “on the “Reformation of the legislation of Societe Anonymes” and by the special 
provisions of L. 4364/2016 “Adaptation of the Greek legislation to Directive 2009/138/EC 
of the European Parliament and the Council of November 25, 2009 regarding the 
underwriting and pursuit of insurance and reinsurance activities (Solvency II),  in Art. 2 and 
8 of Directive 2014/51/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of April 16, 2014, 
regarding the amendment of the Directives 2003/71/EC and 2009/138/EC and Regulations 
(EC) No. 1060/2009, (EU), No. 1094/2010 and (EU) No. 1095/2010, regarding the powers

of the European Insurance and Occupational Pensions Authority (hereinafter EIOPA) and

2. Generally Accepted Accounting Principles 
2.1 Basis for the Presentation of the Financial Statements 
The present financial statements of the Parent Company and the Group have been 
prepared according to the International Financial Reporting Standards (IFRS), as they have 
been issued by the International Accounting Standards Council, adopted by the European 
Union, based on Regulation No. 1606/2002 of the European Council and the Council of the 
European Union of July 19, 2002 and apply for fiscal years beginning after January 1, 2020. 
The preparation of the financial statements according to the I.F.R.S. requires the 
conduction of estimates and adoption of assumptions, that may affect the accounting 
balances of assets and liabilities, the required disclosures for future receivables and 
payables at the date of preparation of the financial statements, as well as the amounts of 
income and expenses recognized during the accounting year. The usage of the available 
financial information and the implementation of a subjective judgments are integral parts 
of the conduction of estimates. The actual future results may differ from the above 
estimations, and any deviations may have a significant impact on the financial statements. 
The most significant estimates of the period are presented in Note 3. 
2.2 Going Concern 
The financial statements have been prepared based on the going concern, which was found 
appropriated by the Board of Directors, without any material uncertainties, by evaluating 
the following: 
 
In 2020, the Covid-19 pandemic hit the fiscal activity in Greece and its return to positive 
growth prospect is intrinsically connected with the course of the pandemic. The Group 
achieved high after-tax profitability, equal to € 18,526 thous., in fiscal year 2020, and the 
earned insurance premiums amounted to € 220,279 thous., increased by 6.0% comparing 
to 2019, despite the unstable, macroeconomic environment that Covid-19 pandemic 
created in fiscal year 2020.  
 
The speed of the treatment of the new genetically modified virus and the vaccination of 
the population, already in action, are important factors for the of the business activities. 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
121 
 
 
It is worth noting that the credit rating agencies appear very optimistic on the estimated of 
the Greek economy, forecasting for 2021, 3.5% to 5% growth, and according to the State 
Budget of 2021, the actual GDP is expected to present 4.8% growth rate, comparing to 
2020.  
 
During the recovery of the lost dynamic, we expect the contribution of all components of 
the domestic demand, less public consumption, which is expected to decrease by 1.9%, 
comparing to the 2.1% increase in 2020, due to the high expenses for the treatment of the 
Covid-19 pandemic. 
 
Management monitors systematically the capital adequacy of the Company, according to 
Solvency II Directive, and proceeds to the required actions for the maintaining of a strong 
capital base and high quality in the investment portfolio. 
 
2.3 Basic Accounting Principles 
 
The same accounting principles and calculation methods have been used in the present 
financial statements, such as the published annual financial statements of the Group for 
the fiscal year ended on December 31, 2019. The new and revised accounting standards 
and interpretations, amendments of standards and interpretations implemented in the 
current or future fiscal years, are attached in Note 2.3.1 and 2.4. 
 
Where this has been found necessary, the comparative figures have been readjusted to 
comply with the changes in the presentation of the current fiscal year (Note 10.7). 
 
The financial statements of the Group and the corporate financial statements of the Parent 
Company as of December 31, 2020 (“the financial statements”) have been prepared 
according to the International Financial Reporting Standards (IFRS) (hereinafter “I.F.R.S.”), 
as adopted by the European Union. The adopted by the E.U. I.F.R.S. may differ than those 
issued by the International Accounting Standards Board (IASB), if the new or amended 
I.F.R.S. are not adopted by the E.U.  
 
The amounted are expressed in euro (€), which is the Company and the Group’s functional 
currency. Any variances in the accumulations of funds are due to rounding. 
 
The financial statements have been prepared according to the historical cost principle, with 
the exception of the available for sale investment portfolio, the financial instruments at fair 
value through profit & loss, the liabilities measured at fair value through profit and loss 
(Unit Linked), the Land and Property from Tangible fixed assets, Real Estate Investments”, 
Participations in related undertakings and technical provisions, that are measured at fair 
value.
