The preparation of the financial statements according to the I.F.R.S. requires the 
conduction of estimates and assumptions, which may affect the accounting balances of the 
assets and liabilities, the required disclosures for possible receivables and payables at the 
date of preparation of the financial statements, as well as the income and expenses 
identified at the reporting period.  
 
The usage of the available information and the conduction of estimates and assumptions 
in the implementation of the accounting principles are integral parts for the conduction of 
estimates in the following areas: Provisions for impairment of the value of loans and other 
receivables, impairment of the financial instruments non traded in an active market, 
including derivatives non traded on a regulated derivative market and a few debt securities, 
impairment of the value of the investment portfolio, estimate of the payables for benefits 
to personnel after leaving the company, insurance liabilities, measurements of real estate 
and participations in related undertakings (read Note 3). The actual future results may 
differ with the estimates of these Financial Statements. 
 
2.3. 1. Changes in the Accounting Principles  
The Group adopted the amendment of I.F.R.S. 16 “Leases” - Decreases of leases due to 
Covid-19 pandemic issued in May 2020. The amendment introduces a practical solution for 
leases in which the Group is the lessee. In these leases, in which the practical solution is 
applied, the Group is not required to estimate whether the decreases of leases are a direct 
consequence of the Covid-19 pandemic are amendments of leases. The Group has applied 
retroactively the amendment and there is no impact in the retained earnings of January 1, 
2020. The implementation of the amendment did not have a significant impact on the 
Financial Statements. 
Therefore, the Group adopted the amendments to IFRS 3: Business merger. 
Based on these changes, the financial entity will define whether a transaction or another 
event is a business merger for the implementation of the definition of IFRS 3, which 
requires the acquired assets and the undertaken liabilities, without consisting a business.  
If the acquired assets do not consist a business, the aforementioned financial entity shall 
account the transaction or any other event as an acquisition of an asset. The standard 
provides guidance for the identification of the business mergers and the definition of a 
business. The impact from the implementation of an amendment is presented in Note 7.7. 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
123 
 
2.4 New standard, interpretations and amendments of current standards 
 
a) Changes of the accounting policies and disclosures 
The accounting policies that were adopted comply with those that were adopted in the 
fiscal year ending on 31/12/2019, except for the standards that the Company and the 
Group have adopted on January 1, 2020, referred below.  
• Amendments to the Conceptual Framework for Financial Reporting, including the 
amendments to the references in the Conceptual Framework within the I.F.R.S. 
In March 2018, the International Accounting Standard Board issued the revised Conceptual 
Framework. This revised framework replaces the previous issuance of the framework, 
which was issued in 2010. The revisions that took place by the Council refer to directions 
on the concept of measurement, presentation, disclosure and derecognition. Moreover, 
the revisions include new definitions on the assets, liabilities and the identification criteria, 
as well as clarifications on other significant fields.  
At the same time, the revised Conceptual Framework, the IASB issued an accompanying 
document under the title “Amendments to the References in the Conceptual Framework 
within the I.F.R.S.”, which includes the following changes in the standards, so that they refer 
to the new Framework. 
The adoption of the amendments does not impact the Financial Statements of the Group. 
• Amendments to I.F.R.S. 3 “Business merger”  
The International Accounting Standard Board issued amendments on the definition of the 
business in the “Business Mergers”, of I.F.R.S. 3, in order to assist the financial entities to 
define whether the total of the acquired activities and assets consist a business or not. 
These amendments clarify the minimum requirements for the definition of the business, 
abolish the evaluation on whether the participants in the market may replace the missing 
information, add direction to assist the financial entities to evaluate, if the required 
procedures are considered significant, limit the definitions of a business and its outflows 
and import a new preliminary fair value concentration test.  
The Group acquired during the fiscal year the Company AEM Energy Systems Private 
Company and the acquisitions was treated as an acquisition of an asset and not as a 
business (see Note 7.7). 
• Amendments of IAS 1 and IAS 8: Definition of the meaning of Material  
The amendments of IAS 1 “Presentation of the Financial Statements” and IAS 8 “Accounting 
Principles and methods, Changes in Accounting Estimates and Errors” aim to align all 
standards with the definition of the Material and clarify certain of its aspects. The new 
definition refers that “an information is considered material, if its omission, inaccuracy or 
concealment is expected to affect these decisions that the main users of the financial 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
124 
 
statements made, based on which, they provide financial information on a specific financial 
entity”. The amendments clarify that the materiality will depend on the nature and size of 
the information or both.  
The adoption of the amendments does not have an impact on the Financial Statements of 
the Group. 
• Adjustment of the Interest Rates: Amendments to IFRS 9, IAS 39 and IFRS 7  
On September 2019, the International Accounting Standard Board issued amendments to 
IFRS 9 “Financial Instruments”, IAS 39 “Financial Instruments: Recognition and 
Measurement” and I.F.R.S. 7 “Financial Instruments: Disclosures”, to handle the 
consequences in certain requirements that are related to the hedge accounting, that derive 
from the uncertainty that is created by the adjustment of interest rates in the market 
(referred to as “IBOR adjustment”). The result of the IBOR adjustments, may be 
uncertainties related to: a) the interest rate that is defined as a hedged risk and/or b) the 
time or the amount of cash flows, based on the benchmark interest rates of the items that 
are hedged or via the hedging instrument, in the period after the replacement of a 
benchmark interest rate standards with alternative interest rate benchmark, almost “Risk 
Free Rate -RFR”). The amendments provide temporary discharge from the possible 
consequences of the uncertainty in this period, by modifying special criteria of the hedge 
accounting, according to IAS 39 or I.F.R.S. 9. These temporary discharges are related mostly 
with the requirement on a very high possibility of fulfillment, regarding the cash flows that 
are hedged, the compliance with the criterion of the discrete nature of the constituent risk 
components and with the implementation of the future and retroactive evaluation of the 
effectiveness of a hedge accounting relationship. 
The IASB handles the adjustment of the IBOR and the possible consequences in the financial 
information in two phases. These amendments complete the first phase, that focused on 
issues that affect the financial information in the period before the adjustment of the 
reference rates. The second phase focuses on the possible matter that may arise when the 
current interest rates are replaced by the RFR.  
The adoption of the amendments does not have an impact on the Financial Statements of 
the Group. 
b) Standards that have been issued but do not apply in the present accounting period and 
were not previously adopted: 
The Group has not adopted any of the following standards, interpretations or amendments 
that have been issued but do not apply in the present accounting period. Furthermore, the 
Group is in the process of assessment of all standards and interpretations or amendments 
that have been issued but did not apply in the current period and decided that there will 
not be any significant impact on the financial statements from their implementation, except 
for IFRS 17, for which the Group does not expect significant consequences, due to the 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
125 
 
nature of its operations, which have not yet been defined. 
• 
IAS 1 (Amendment) “Classification of liabilities as short-term or long-term” 
(effective in the annual accounting periods beginning on or after January 1, 2023):  
The amendments apply for annual accounting periods beginning on or after January 1, 
2022, and they may also apply earlier. The IASB issued a draft standard on the 
postponement of the effective date of the amendments, which shall apply on January 1, 
2023. These amendments aim to achieve consistency in the implementation of the 
requirements of the standard, helping companies determine, how much the borrowing and 
other requirements with uncertain settlement date are classified as short-term or long-
term liabilities, in the Statement of Financial Position. These amendments affect the 
presentation of the liabilities in the Statement of Financial Position, and they do not amend 
the current requirements on the measurement or time for recognition of an asset, a 
liability, income or expenses or the disclosures on these assets. Furthermore, the 
amendments clarify the requirements for classifications on the borrowing, that a Company 
may arrange through the issuance of equities.  
The amendments have not yet been adopted by the European Union. 
• IFRS 9, IAS 39, IFRS 7, IFRS 4 and IFRS 16 (Amendments) “Reformation of the reference 
interests - Phase 2” (effective for the annual accounting period beginning on or after 
January 1, 2021).  
On August 2020, the International Accounting Standard Board issued amendments to 
I.F.R.S. 9, IAS 39, I.F.R.S. 7, I.F.R.S. 4 and IFRS. 16 that complete its operations on the 
consequences of the reformation of the interbanking borrowing rate in the financial 
information. The amendments provide temporary facilitation, which handle the 
consequences in the financial information when an interbanking borrowing rate is replaced 
with an alternative risk free rate. More specifically, these amendments provide a practical 
facilitation on the accounting management of the changes in the basis of the determination 
of the contractual cash flows of financial assets and liabilities, requiring the adjustment of 
the actual interest rate, as also in the case of change in the market interest rate. 
Furthermore, the amendments import facilitations for the non-interruption of the hedging 
relationships, including a temporary facilitation from the requirement of a discrete 
recognition of an alternate risk free interest rate, that is defined as hedging of a risk 
element. Therefore, these amendments in I.F.R.S. 4 allow the insurance companies to 
follow and implement IAS 39, receive the same facilitations with those provided in the 
amendments of I.F.R.S. 9. The amendments import to I.F.R.S. 7 “Financial Instruments: 
Disclosures”, additional disclosures that allow the readers of the financial statements to 
understand the consequences of the reformation of the interbanking borrowing rates in 
the financial instruments and the strategic risk management of the financial entity. The 
amendments apply for the annual accounting periods beginning on or after January 1, 
2021, and they may also apply earlier. The amendments do not require from the entity to 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
126 
 
reform the previous reporting periods. 
• IFRS 10 (Amendment) Consolidated Financial Statements and IAS 28 Investments in 
Associated Companies and Joint Ventures: Sale or Contribution of assets between an 
investor and the associated Company or Joint Venture:  
The amendments face a recognized inconsistency between the requirements of I.F.R.S 10 
and those of IAS 28 for the treatment of the sale or the contribution of assets between the 
investor and the associated company or joint venture. The main consequence of the 
amendments is that a comprehensive profit or loss is recognized when the transaction 
includes a business (subsidiary or not). A part of the profit or loss is recognized when the 
transaction includes assets that do not consist a business, even if these instruments are 
part of a subsidiary. On December 2015, the IASB postponed indefinitely the effective date 
of this amendment, expecting the result of the work for the equity method. The 
amendments have not yet been adopted by the European Union. 
• IFRS 3 Business Mergers, IAS 16, Tangible Assets, IAS 37 Provisions, possible liabilities 
and possible assets and Annual Upgrades 2018-2020 I.F.R.S. (Amendments):  
The amendments apply for the annual accounting periods starting on or after January 1, 
2022, and they may also apply earlier. IASB issued, to a limited scope, amendments to 
standards, as below:  
— IFRS 3 Business mergers:  The amendments update a reference in the I.F.R.S. 3 in 
the Conceptual Framework on the Financial Reporting, without change in the 
accounting requirements of the standard for business mergers. 
— IAS Fixed Assets: The amendment prohibits the decrease of the cost of the fixed 
assets, with amounts that are estimated from the sale of assets produced, while the 
company prepares the asset for the future use. The income from the sales and the 
related cost are recognized in the financial results. 
— IAS 37 Provisions, possible liabilities and possible assets: The amendments define 
the expenses for the fulfillment of a contract, within the assessment of a contract, 
if this is burdensome. 
 
Small amendments were conducted in the Annual Reformations 2018-2020 in IFRS 1 - First 
Implementation of the International Financial Reporting Standards, IFRS 9 Financial 
Instruments, IAS 41 -Agriculture and the indicative examples that accompany IFRS 16-
Leases. 
• IFRS 17 “Insurance Contracts” and Amendments to IFRS 17 (effective for annual 
accounting periods starting on or after January 1, 2023):  
IFRS 17 was issued on May 2017 and along with the amendments to IFRS 17 issued on June 
2020, it replaces IFRS 4. IFRS 17 establishes the principles for the recognition, measurement 
and presentation of insurance contracts that are in the implementation stage of the 
standard, as long as the relevant disclosures. The objective of this standards is to ensure 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
127 
 
that an entity provides relevant information, that present a fair image of these contracts. 
The new standard will solve problems of comparability created by I.F.R.S. 4, since it requires 
for all insurance contracts to enter in the accounts in a consistent way. The insurance 
obligations shall be measured at current values and not at historical cost. This standard has 
not been yet adopted by the E.U. At the present time period, the Group proceeds to all 
necessary actions to comply with this standard. 
 
• IFRS 9 “Financial Instruments”:  
The final edition of IFRS 9 replaces IAS 39, “Financial Instruments - Recognition and 
measurement”. This standard introduces new requirements on the classification, 
measurement, impairment and accounting hedging. Given the fact that the main activity of 
European Reliance General Insurance Co. S.A. is the provision of Insurance services with 
over 97% of its liabilities being connected with the insurance activity, the requirements for 
the change of the effective date of the IFRS 9, along with IFRS 17 are met, i.e. change of the 
date on 1 January, 2023. 
 
• Amendments to IFRS 4 (Postponement of the effective date of IFRS 9) 
In the current phase, according to the IFRS 4 Insurance Contracts the present effective date 
of IFRS 9 is January 1, 2021, up to which a temporary exclusion is provided. The Draft Report 
for the Amendments to IFRS 17 issued on May 2019, proposed the postponement of the 
temporary exclusion of IFRS 9, for one year. In continuance, based on the re-examinations 
of IASB, the effective date of IFRS 9 was further postponed on January 1, 2023, in order to 
align with the effective date of IFRS 17 Insurance Contracts. IASB issued a postponement 
of the temporary exclusion of the implementation IFRS 9 (Amendments to IFRS 4) on June 
25, 2020. As a result, EFRAG issued a consultive letter of approval on the amendments. The 
total estimate of EFAG is that the amendments should meet the criteria for approval for 
use in the EU and, therefore, their approval is suggested. 
 
2.5 Consolidation 
Subsidiaries are entities in which the Parent Company had directly or indirectly control. The 
parent company controls the subsidiary when the Parent company is exposed or is entitled 
to various benefits from its correlation to one company and has the ability to affect these 
benefits, through its power on the subsidiary.  
 
The Parent Company is required to consolidate all its subsidiaries from the date of their 
acquisition, and the consolidation obligation ceases to exist the date that the control of 
these ceases. The consolidated financial statements include the following subsidiaries:

Where necessary, the financial statements of these subsidiaries were amended in order to 
be consistent with the accounting principles applied by the Parent Company. Intercompany 
receivables- liabilities, intercompany income- expenses and intercompany profit - losses 
were written off to determine the actual amount of the Group’s assets and financial results. 
 
2.5.1 Consolidation principles 
 
The consolidated financial statements include the financial statements of the Parent 
Company and its subsidiaries, which are controlled by the Parent Company. The control 
applies where the Parent Company has the power to steer the financial and business 
policies of its subsidiaries, so as to obtain benefits from their activities. The revenues, 
expenses and other comprehensive income of the subsidiaries that are acquired or sold 
during the year are incorporated in the consolidated statement statement of financial 
results and consolidated statement of comprehensive income from the date of acquisition 
of the subsidiaries and cease to be incorporated when the subsidiaries are sold. The net 
profit and total comprehensive income of the subsidiaries are distributed among the 
shareholders of the Parent Company and the minority interests even if with this allocation, 
the minority interests’ balance becomes negative. If the subsidiaries apply different 
accounting principles than those of the Group applies, necessary adjustments are made to 
their financial statements, in order to provide consistency with the accounting principles of 
the Group. All intercompany transactions, intercompany balances and intercompany 
income and expenses are eliminated on consolidation. 
 
2.5.2 Minority interests 
 
Minority interests are initially measured either at fair value or at the proportionate share 
of the net identifiable assets. This option is conducted per transaction. After the acquisition, 
the book value of the minority interests is the value of such, at the initial recognition, plus 
the share of the minority interests in the subsequent changes in the equity. The total 
comprehensive income is distributed to minority interests, even if this has as a result a debit 
balance in the minority interests. 
 
2.5.3 Changes in the ownership percentage of the subsidiaries that do not result in loss of 
control 
 
Changes in ownership percentage in a subsidiary that do not result to loss of control, are 
entered as transactions performed between shareholders. The registered balance of the 
Parent company’s “owner equity of shareholders account” and the “Minority interests” 
account are readjusted in order to reflect the change in the shareholders’ ownership 
percentage in the subsidiary. Any difference between the adjustment of the minority 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
129 
 
interests and the fair value of the consideration paid or received is entered directly in the 
equity that is attributable to shareholders of the Parent company. 
 
2.5.4 Loss of control 
 
If the change results in loss of control of the subsidiary, the profit or loss from the sale is 
calculated as the difference between (i) the sum of the fair value of the consideration 
received and the fair value of the participation in the subsidiary and (ii) the pre-sale book 
value of the assets (including the goodwill), and the minority rights. The unrealized profits 
or losses that were recognized in other comprehensive income and result from the fair 
value measurement of the subsidiary’s assets are accounted as if the Parent company had 
directly sold off the assets (transfer to the financial results statement or transfer to profits 
carried forward, in accordance with the applicable IFRS). The fair value of ownership 
interest in a subsidiary, which continues to exist after the date of loss of control, is the fair 
value at the initial recognition, in accordance with IAS 39, or the acquisition cost, if it is 
recognized as associate or joint venture, in accordance with the applicable standards. 
 
2.5.5 Contribution of assets to a subsidiary by exchange of shares issued by the subsidiary 
 
If the Parent Company contributes with tangible fixed assets, intangible assets or 
investment properties to an existing or newly established subsidiary in exchange for shares 
issued by the subsidiary, the Parent Company registers in its corporate financial statements 
its ownership value in the book value of the contributed intangible assets. Such transactions 
do not have any impact on the consolidated financial statements. 
 
2.5.6 Associate companies 
 
With the exception of special conditions, an associate company is the company in which 
the Group holds 20% to 50% of the voting rights and it has a certain influence, but no 
control. The investments in associate companies are incorporated in the financial 
statements through the equity method. Under the equity method, investments in 
associates are initially entered at the acquisition cost. The goodwill that arises at the 
acquisition of an associated company is included in the acquisition cost of the investment 
(net of accumulated impairment losses - if any). The value of the investment is increased 
or decreased depending on the share of the Group in profit or losses of the associate 
company following the acquisition (and is recognized in the Group’s statement of 
comprehensive income) and from the reserve activities (and is recognized in the Group’s 
reserves). The amount of dividends received by the associate company in the fiscal year, 
reduces the book value of this investment. Investments in associate companies that were 
realized for the sole purpose of being sold within one year since the date of purchase, i.e. 
when the Group’s substantial influence is deemed temporary, are recorded as "non-current 
assets held for sale". Unrealized profits from transactions between the Group and the 
associate companies are eliminated to the extent of the Group's ownership interest in 
these companies. Any such losses, which are also eliminated, constitute an indication of 
the impairment of the value of the transferred asset. Where necessary, the financial 
information of the associate companies used during the application of the equity method 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
130 
 
were amended, in order to ensure the consistency with the accounting principles adopted 
by the Group. 
 
2.5.7 Joint Ventures 
 
A joint venture is defined as an entity in which the Group exercises control jointly with other 
parties. The Group’s rights in joint ventures are accounted according to the equity method. 
 
2.5.8 Investments in subsidiaries, associate companies and joint ventures in the corporate 
financial statements 
 
The subsidiaries, associate companies, joint mergers are measured in the financial 
statements of the Parent company at fair value through profit and loss, as indicatively 
referred to in section 2.9.3. Since the shares of these companies are not listed on a 
regulated market, their valuation is performed at fair value using various valuation 
methods, such as the Price to book ratio, the Method of the Current Stock exchange price 
of the share / Earning per Share, Discounted Cash Flow, DCF) and Method of the Company 
/Pre-tax Profit, Financial and Investment Results (Enterprise Value/ Earnings Before 
Interest, tax, Depreciation & Amortization, EV/ EBITDA). The above investments are 
classified in Tier 3 as the valuation of fair value is based on the non-observable data that 
are supported by few or zero transactions on a regulated market and have a significant 
impact on the fair value. If the valuation of an asset uses observable data that required 
significant adjustments based on non-observable data, this instrument is classified at level 
3. This level includes financial instruments the value of which is calculated using valuation 
models, advance prepayment of cash flows or similar techniques, as well as instruments 
for which the determination of the fair value requires decision or assessment by the 
Management. 
 
2.6 Business merger 
 
2.6.1 Acquisition method 
 
Business acquisitions falling within the scope of IFRS 3 are entered with the acquisition 
method. The consideration paid in a business merger is measured at fair value, which is 
calculated as the sum of the fair values at the acquisition date of the assets contributed by 
the Group, of liabilities undertaken by the Group to the former owners of the acquired 
business and equity issued by the Group in exchange for the control on the acquired 
business. The expenses relating to the acquisition are entered in the financial results 
statement. At the acquisition date, the assets acquired and the liabilities undertaken are 
entered at the fair value as at the acquisition date, except for deferred tax claims and 
liabilities or assets related to employee benefits which are entered in accordance with IAS 
12 "Income Tax" and IAS 19 “Employee Benefits”, respectively, liabilities or equity related 
to benefit plans of the acquired business that depend on the value of shares or benefit 
plans of the Group that were concluded to replace the benefits plans of the acquired 
business that depend on the value of the shares registered in accordance with IFRS. 2 
Benefits that depend on the value of Shares at the acquisition date, and assets (or groups 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
131 
 
of assets) held for sale in accordance with IFRS 5 “Non-current Assets Held for Sale and 
Discontinued Operations” are entered in accordance with this standard. 
 
2.6.2 Goodwill 
 
Goodwill is entered as the excess amount between (a) the sum of the consideration paid, 
any minority interests in the acquired business and the fair value of any previous 
participation in the acquired business and (b) the net asset value as at the acquisition date 
of the assets acquired and liabilities undertaken. If, after the review, the net value of the 
assets acquired and liabilities assumed as at the acquisition date, exceed the sum of the 
paid consideration, any minority interests in the acquired business and the fair value of any 
previous participation in the acquired business, the difference is recorded directly in the 
statement of financial results. The Company conducts audit for any impairment at each 
date of the financial reporting. 
 
2.6.3 Contingent consideration 
 
If the price agreed upon during the business merger includes assets or liabilities resulting 
from contingent consideration, the contingent consideration is measured at fair value on 
the acquisition date and is included in the total consideration at the business merger. 
Changes in the fair value of the contingent consideration, which are included in the 
adjustments of the period of finalizing the acquisition, are retroactively recognized with a 
corresponding adjustment of the goodwill. The adjustments in the period of acquisition of 
the finalization arise from new information obtained in this period (which may not exceed 
one year since the acquisition date) relating to facts and circumstances that existed at the 
acquisition date. Changes in the fair value of the contingent consideration that are not 
included in the adjustments of the acquisition finalization period are accounted based on 
the classification of the contingent consideration. When the contingent consideration is 
classified as an equity, it is not re-measured at following reporting periods and its 
subsequent liquidation is recognized in the equity. If the contingent consideration is 
classified as an asset or liability, it is re-measured in subsequent reporting periods, in 
accordance with IAS 39 or IAS 37 and the result of the valuation (profit or loss) is recognized 
in the statement of the financial results. 
 
2.6.4 Business merger in successive stages 
 
When the business merger proceeds in stages, the participation of the Group held in 
acquired business by the Group is valued at fair value as at the acquisition date (the date in 
which the Group acquires control) and the resulting profit or loss is recognized in the 
financial results statement. Amounts recognized in other comprehensive income and which 
were generated by the acquired business before the acquisition of its control are 
recognized in the financial results statement, provided that this is the appropriate handling 
in the event of purchase.  
 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
132 
 
2.6.5 Provisional accounting 
 
If the values related to the initial entry of a business merger are not finalized by the date of 
preparation of the financial statements for the period in which the business merger occurs, 
the Group enters provisional values for these amounts. The provisional amounts are 
adjusted during the finalization of the acquisition (see Note 2.5.3 above), or additional 
assets or the liabilities are recognized to reflect new information about facts and 
circumstances that existed at the acquisition date, which would affect the amounts 
recognized on that date, had they been known. 
 
2.7 Transactions in foreign currency 
 
The data contained in the financial statements are expressed in euro (€), which is the 
Company and the Group’s functional currency. 
There are no other significant assets and liabilities in other currencies. 
Transactions in foreign currencies are translated into the functional currency using the 
foreign exchange rates that applied at the dates of the transactions.  
Foreign exchange profit and losses resulting from the settlement of such transactions and 
from the conversion of monetary assets and liabilities in foreign currencies are recognized 
in the Statement of Total Comprehensive Income.  
The foreign exchange differences from the conversion of securities and other monetary 
financial assets that are re- measured at fair value are included in the Statement of Total 
Comprehensive Income.  
The non-monetary financial assets that are measured at foreign currency and are 
measured at fair value, are converted based on the foreign exchange rate at the date of 
determination of the fair value.  
The foreign exchange differences are recognized in the Statement of Total Comprehensive 
Income. 
 
2.8 Asset - Liabilities Offsetting 
 
The offsetting of financial assets with liabilities and the presentation of the net amount in 
the Financial Statements is performed only if there is a legal right to offset the recorded 
amounts and there is an intention to settle the net amount obtained from the offsetting, 
for the simultaneous settlement of the total amount of the financial asset and the liabilities. 
 
2.9 Financial assets and liabilities at fair value through profit or loss 
 
This category has two sub-categories: 
- Trading portfolio and 
- Financial assets and liabilities that have been designated at fair value through profit or 
loss 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
133 
 
 
2.9.1 Trading portfolio 
 
The trading portfolio includes securities that are acquired either to generate profit from 
short-term variation in price or the dealer's margin or are securities that belong in a 
securities portfolio for which there is a history of short-term profit. Derivatives also belong 
to the trading portfolio, unless they have been designated as and are effective hedging 
instruments. The trading portfolio may include securities subject to sale and repurchase 
agreement. 
 
2.9. 2 Financial assets and liabilities that have been designated at fair value through profit 
or loss 
 
Upon initial recognition, the Group determines any financial asset or liability as a financial 
instrument measured at fair value through profit or loss when: 
 
a) It eliminates or significantly reduces a measurement or recognition inconsistency 
(sometimes referred to as an “accounting mismatch”) that would arise if the related 
derivative financial products were accounted for as a trading portfolio and the underlying 
financial instruments were measured at amortized cost, such as loans and claims against 
insured and financial institutions or liabilities from debt securities. 
 
b) A group of financial assets, liabilities or both that is managed, and its performance is 
evaluated on a fair value basis, in accordance with the documented risk management or 
investment management strategy, and the information on the group is provided internally 
at this level to the Group’s management, for example to the BoD or the CEO. 
 
c) Financial instruments containing one or more embedded derivatives, the existence of 
which significantly modifies the cash flows and therefore should be shown in separate 
database, if they were not classified in this category. The determination of a financial asset 
at fair value through profit or loss is irrevocable. 
 
2.9.3 Measurement 
The financial assets and liabilities measured at fair value in the financial results (trading 
portfolio and defined financial assets recognized directly at fair value in the financial 
results) are initial recognized at fair value and later measures at fair value. 
The realized gains and losses resulting from the provision or acquisition of the above assets 
and the unrealized gains or losses arising from the changes at fair value, are included in the 
“Results of financial acts and investment portfolio securities”. 
The income from dividends is recognized in the statements of financial results in the 
account “Net other income/ (expenses)”, when the right to collect the dividend has been 
established and this is the approval date of the dividends by the General meeting of 
Shareholders. 
The amount of variance in the period and cumulatively, at the fair values of the financial 
liabilities and loans and receivables, which is attributed to the changes of the credit risk is 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
134 
 
calculated as the amount of change of fair value, which is not attributed to changes in 
market conditions causing increase of market risk. 
 
2.10 Investment portfolio 
The Group classifies its investments as available-for-sale and held-to-maturity financial 
assets. The classification of the investments is decided upon acquisition. The Group does 
not hold on 31/12/2020 financial assets held to maturity. 
 
Initial Recognition  
The available for sale investments are initially recorded at fair value, which is increased by 
the transaction costs.  
The held to maturity investment securities include securities that are not derivatives but 
are traded on regulated markets, with fixed or determinable payments and fixed maturities 
for which there is the intention and ability by the Group’s management to hold to maturity 
date. 
 
Future measurement of financial assets 
Subsequent to the purchase date, available for sale securities are measured at fair value. 
The fair value is individually determined pursuant to the values provided by the regulated 
markets of securities trading or by models for advance prepayment of future cash flows. 
Profits and losses arising from variations in the fair value of available for sale securities are 
recorded in owners’ equity, until these securities are sold or disposed in any other way, at 
which time the corresponding profits/losses are transferred to the profit and loss account. 
 
The held to maturity investment securities are registered at their depreciated value by 
applying the effective interest rate method, less any provision for their impairment. 
Moreover, the calculation of the depreciable value of held to maturity securities includes 
the premium or discount on capital stock that arose on the date of acquisition, plus 
transaction expenses, commissions and paid or received basis points. 
 
Impairment  
An investment in a security is considered to be impaired when the book value exceeds the 
recoverable amount and there are indications that the decline in the value has reached a 
level that makes the invested capital infeasible to recover in the near future. This mostly 
occurs when the decrease of the fair value below the cost is significant and prolonged. In 
this case the relative reserve is transferred to the financial results statement. The income 
from interest and dividends on available for sale assets are recorded in the statement of 
financial results. 
 
Derecognition 
A financial instrument is not reflected in the financial statements of the Group from the 
moment that Group ceases to have control over the contractual rights that derive from the 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
135 
 
financial instrument. The control on the rights of the financial instrument ceases to exist 
when it is sold or when its relevant cash inflows are transferred to an independent third 
party. 
 
2.11 Reclassification of financial instruments 
 
The Group transfers non-derivative securities from the trading portfolio or the available for 
sale portfolio to the loans and receivables portfolio, provided the securities meet the 
definition of that category at the date of transfer and the Group has the intention and 
ability to hold the securities for the foreseeable future or until maturity. Where rare 
circumstances cause a significant deterioration of the trading activity or greatly influence 
the market prices of the trading portfolio’s non-derivative assets, the Group transfers these 
assets from the trading portfolio to the held-to-maturity or available-for-sale portfolio, 
provided that the assets meet the definition of the corresponding category at the date of 
transfer and the Group does not intend on selling them soon. Such transfers may be made 
only once for each rare circumstance. If there is a change in its intention or ability to 
withheld a security to maturity, the Group will transfer these securities from the available 
for sale category to the held to maturity category, if the instruments meet the definition of 
the latter on the date of transfer. For financial instruments that are transferred as stated 
above, the fair value as at the date of transfer becomes the new depreciated cost as at that 
date. When instruments being transferred from the trading portfolio contain embedded 
derivatives at the date of transfer, the Group reassesses if the embedded derivatives need 
to be separated from the main contract based on the conditions prevailing when the Group 
acquired the asset. 
 
The Group transfers securities that have been transferred to loans and receivables from 
the trading portfolio, or the available for sale portfolio to the available for sale portfolio, if 
the securities are subsequently traded on a regulated market and the Group does not 
intend to hold them for the foreseeable future or until maturity. The fair value of the 
securities on the transfer date becomes the new amortized cost as at that date. The 
difference between the amortized cost immediately prior to transfer and the fair value on 
the date of transfer is recognized in the available- for sale securities reserve through the 
statement of other comprehensive income and it is depreciated in the statement of 
financial results. 
 
2.12 Fair value of the financial assets 
 
The Group calculates the fair value of the financial instruments based on a fair value 
calculation framework that classifies the financial instruments in a three-level hierarchy of 
data that are used in the valuation as described below. 
 
Tier 1:  
Stock market prices on an active market of identical financial instruments. 
Level 1 includes securities, shares and derivatives traded on an active market. 
An active market is the market in which transactions are of sufficient 
frequency and volume so that the information on the price is provided on a 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
136 
 
going basis and is characterized by low profit margins. 
 
Tier 2:  
Observed data other than the prices of Level 1, such as stock prices of similar 
instruments, market prices that are not active, or other data that is observable 
or can be confirmed by observable data (for example, prices that derive from 
observable data) for almost the total of the duration of the instrument. Level 
2 includes securities with prices on non-active markets, as well as non-
marketable securities and derivatives whose values are calculated using 
valuation models, cash flow discounts or similar techniques with data that are 
observable on the market or can be calculated or confirmed by observable 
data from the market. This category generally includes government and 
corporate bonds with prices on markets that are not active and some over 
the-counter derivatives.  
 
Tier 3:  
Unobservable data supported by few or no transactions in an active market 
and significantly affecting fair value. If the valuation of an asset uses 
observable data that required significant adjustments based on non-
observable data, this instrument is classified at level 3. This level includes 
financial instruments the value of which is calculated using valuation models, 
advance prepayment of cash flows or similar techniques, as well as 
instruments for which the determination of the fair value requires decision or 
assessment by the Management. 
 
The level in the hierarchy of fair value, in which a fair value calculation is classified, is 
determined based on the lower level of data that are used in the calculation of the fair 
value and have a significant effect. For this purpose, the importance of a data is evaluated 
in relation to the total fair value. 
 
2.13 Insurance Contracts 
 
The Company issues insurance policies that expose it to the underwriting risk, financial risk, 
or combination of these risks. The Company has adopted IFRS 4 and the portfolio has been 
reclassified, as follows: 
 
Separation of financial risks of the insurance policies 
For policies that contain both insurance and financial risk, the Company does not separate 
the financial risk, when the insurance risk is considered significant. For the measurement 
of the significance of the insurance risk, the ratio of the death fund to the acquisition value 
is audited. In case that, even once in the development period of the contract, the ratio is 
105%, the product will be classified as insurance product. 
 
(a) Insurance policies 
The Insurance policies are classified in three categories depending on the nature of the 
insured risk. 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
137 
 
 
i. Life Insurance Policies 
The Life insurance policies provide insurance coverage to events for a long period of time. 
Premiums are recognized as revenue when they become payable by the insured. Premiums 
are presented without the deduction of the relevant commissions. 
This category includes policies issued by the Company to cover the risk of death, pensions, 
disabilities, accidents, sickness, individual and group schemes.  
 
 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
138 
 
 
Additional Coverages in Life and Health Insurance Policies 
 
The Life Insurance policies may include additional coverage for cases of accidents and 
disease, for the insured and the dependents. 
 
Policies for Life Insurance policyholders who bear the investment risk (Unit Linked) 
 
These policies transfer the financial risk to the holder of the policy and at the same time 
the insurance Company undertakes the coverage with guarantee for a part of the financial 
risk.  
Moreover, the policies entail an important underwriting risk (cover for death, disability, 
accident and sickness). The main risks on which the Company is exposed are similar to the 
traditional Life Insurance policies. A sufficiency audit is under process through the 
implementation of an additional methodology similar to the individual traditional life 
policies. 
 
(ii) Group Policies - Deposit Administration Funds 
 
It is the group insurance with which the investment management insurance, cover (deposit 
administration fund) is agreed, in which the insurer’s benefit is paid either with the 
withdrawal of the insured member from the working place, for any reason, or after reaching 
a certain age. The account is managed either by the Company freely or further according 
to the directions of the Policyholder. Moreover, the group policies include a significant 
underwriting risk, and in case of death they provide coverage that exceed the credit balance 
of the account. 
 
(iii) Non-Life Insurance Policies 
 
The products cover part of the total of General insurances for the coverage of personal, 
trading and industrial risks relevant to the loss of property and third party liability, as well 
as Land Vehicles. The recognition of income takes place proportionally with the passing of 
time, during the policy period. 
 
Motor Third Party Liability 
This category includes policies issued by the Company for the coverage of motor third party 
liability and land vehicles risk. 
 
Other Non-Life 
This category includes policies the provide coverage against the risk of fire, earthquake, 
theft, cargo, third party liability insurance, road assistance, hull, etc. The non-life insurance 
policies are recognized as revenues (earned premiums) depending on the duration of the 
insurance policy (policy period). At the date of the financial statements, part of the 
insurance premiums that corresponds to the following year or years for the period from 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
139 
 
the closing of the financial statements until the end of the period for which the premiums 
have been recorded, is included in the reserve of non-earned premiums. Premiums are 
presented without the deduction of the relevant commissions. 
 
(b). Deferred Acquisition Costs 
Commissions and other acquisition costs of new policies and renewals of the following fiscal 
year are presented in the Assets account “Commissions and other expenses of written 
premiums for future years” and are allocated to the fiscal years depending on the length of 
the policy period. 
 
(c) Insurance provisions 
Insurance provisions are the contractual liabilities of the insurance Company, that derive 
from insurance policies. The insurance provisions (mathematical provisions, provisions for 
non-earned premiums, provisions for outstanding claims, benefits payable and unexpired 
risk reserves) are calculated in accordance with the applicable Greek legislation and their 
adequacy is audited separately for each group of policies based on the adequacy test. 
Insurance provisions are analyzed in the following major categories: 
 
Mathematical provisions 
These include the mathematical reserves of life insurance and capitalization policies and 
correspond to the difference that arises during the closing of the financial statements 
between the present value of financial liabilities assumed by the insurance Company for 
every life insurance policy including the reserves of undistributed profit to policyholders 
and the present value of the net written premiums that are due by the insured and are 
payable to the insurance company within the following years. This variance is estimated 
based on the applicable actuarial methods, provided by the Greek Legislation. 
 
Provisions for non-accrued premiums 
They include the rate of gross written premiums that relate to policies paid in advance and 
are in force at the closing date of the financial statements. 
 
Unexpired risk reserve 
It refers to the additional provision, formed at the closing date of the financial statements 
when the reserve of unearned premiums and policy fees, is estimated not sufficient to 
cover the estimated losses and expenses of the insurance policies in force on that date, and 
in that case this provision is not discounted. 
 
Provisions for Outstanding claims 
 
The provisions that are formed at the date of the preparation of the financial statements 
for the full coverage of liabilities and insurance risks that have occurred before the closing 
date of the Statement of Financial Position, regardless of whether these have been 
reported or not, for which the relative indemnity amounts, and relevant expenses have not 
been paid, or the exact amount has not been determined, or the extent of the insurance 
company’s liability is disputed. The amount of the estimated provision is calculated based 
on the available information, such as reports, medical reports, court decisions, at the date 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
140 
 
of preparation of the financial statements. 
 
Payable benefits 
These are insurance benefits due to the insured that for various reasons have not been paid 
by the closing date of the financial statements. 
 
Provisions for life insurance where the policyholders bear the financial risk: 
 
These are provisions that are intended to cover the assumptions of liability of the insurance 
company, which are associated with financial instruments within the framework of the Life 
insurance policies, whose value or performance is determined in relation to the 
investments, for which the policyholder bears the financial risk. They refer to insurance 
policies that are directly associated with Assets (Unit Linked), whose valuation at the 
current value derives from profits or losses that have direct impact to the amount of the 
insurance provisions. In such cases, the insurance company equally adjusts the insurance 
provisions equally with the unrealized profit or losses from the valuation and the arising 
variance of the insurance provisions is transferred to the Statement of Total 
Comprehensive Income. 
 
Insurance Provisions Sufficiency Reserve (Liability Adequacy Test - “LAT”): 
 
The Company evaluates the sufficiency of the recognized insurance provisions, applying the 
sufficiency audit of the insurance payables, using current estimates of the future cash flows 
of the insurance policies. In case that the arising, from the sufficiency audit of the insurance 
reserves, liabilities of the Group, supersede the insurance reserves that have been 
estimated according to the Greek Legislation, the additional provision increases the 
reserves of the sectors that it refers to, and burdens the results of the fiscal year, for which 
the audit is conducted. 
2.14 Receivables against Premiums debtors 
 
Classification 
It included non-derivative financial assets with fixed or determinable payments, which are 
not traded on active money markets and there is no intention for sale. They are included in 
the current assets, except for those with maturity dates longer than 12 months, since the 
date of the closing balance sheet, which are included in the non-current assets. 
 
They refer to the Other Receivables, apart from the Receivables against premium debtors.   
 
Recognition and measurement 
The loans and the receivables are measured at their depreciated value by applying the 
effective interest rate method, after deducting any provision for impairment of value. 
 
2.14.1 Receivables against Premium Debtors 
 
Receivables against premium debtors include premiums from insured, agents with right to 

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
141 
 
collect and disputed cases. The Group has drawn up contracts for providing time to agents 
for the collection of premiums. Receivables against premium debtors are recorded in the 
financial statements at the date of issuance of the Insurance Policies. 
 
2.14.1.1 Provisions for impairment of the value of premium debtors 
 
A provision for the impairment of the value of a receivable is formed when there are 
reasonable (objective) grounds that the Group will not collect the total of receivables due, 
according to the policy and its initial terms. The Group assesses whether there are 
reasonable grounds for possible losses in the portfolio of the receivables against the 
insured.  
A receivable is subject to impairment of its value, when, according to the terms of the 
policy, it seems significant delay when there are reasonable grounds, at the valuation date 
that demonstrate that the premium debtors, will not be able to respond to their 
obligations. At every balance sheet date, all delayed or doubtful receivables are assessed 
in order to determine the necessity or not of provision for bad debts. The balance of the 
specific provision for bad debts is accordingly adjusted to each closing date of the balance 
sheet to reflect any possible, relevant risks. Each balance write-off is charged to the existing 
provision for bad debts. The Group's policy is to avoid the write-off of any receivables, until 
all possible practical and legal actions have been performed for their collection. 
 
2.15 Reinsurance policies  
 
The part of the reserve that is attributable to the Company's reinsurer is recognized as an 
asset in the "Reinsurance Receivables" account. Other Receivables and Liabilities to the 
reinsurers mainly refer to due reinsurance premiums, and their ratio in paid or outstanding 
claims.  
The Company examines whether the reinsurance receivables have incurred impairment at 
the preparation of the financial statements, it reduces the book value respectively and 
recognizes the impairment loss at the results. 
 
Reinsurance Receivables are impaired if, and only if: 
(i) there is objective evidence, as a result of the event that occurred after the initial 
recognition of the receivable, that the Group may not receive the full amount due under 
the terms of the policy and 
(ii) this event has a reliably measurable impact on the amounts that the Group will 
receive from the reinsurer. 
 
2.16 Tangible fixed assets 
 
Tangible assets include fields and real estate, leasehold improvements, transportation 
means and equipment, which are held by the Group for operational use or for 
administrative purposes. They are initially entered at the acquisition cost, which includes 
all costs required to place a fixed asset into operating status.    
The tangible assets, other than the real estate, are valued at acquisition cost less the 
accumulated depreciations using the straight line depreciation method.  

EUROPEAN RELIANCE GENERAL INSURANCE CO.S.A.                                                      
2020 ANNUAL FINANCIAL REPORT 
 
 
142 
 
Own-occupied properties are measured at readjusted amounts, as determined by 
independent appraisers. The changes are recognized in the equity, when they are credit, 
and any other losses, after the impairment of the relevant reserve, are registered in the 
financial results.  
Expenses incurred after the acquisition of a fixed asset, which is included in the “tangible 
assets” account, are capitalized only when it is likely that these expenses will yield 
additional economic benefits for the Group in the future, beyond those that were initially 
expected at the acquisition of the fixed asset. Otherwise, these costs are transferred 
directly to the profit or loss account at the time of their realization. The depreciation of a 
tangible fixed asset begins when it is available for use and ceases only when the tangible 
fixed asset is sold or transferred. Consequently, the depreciation of a tangible fixed asset 
that ceases to be used is not terminated, unless it is fully depreciated, but rather its useful 
life is reassessed. Tangible assets are depreciated using the straight-line depreciation 
method over their useful lives. The estimated useful life of tangible fixed asset per category 
is as follows: 
Fields 
Non-depreciable 
Buildings 
                                                 (Owner-occupied) up to 40 years 
Leasehold improvements 
                        Over the remaining lease term, not  
exceeding 12 years 
Furniture and other equipment                      up to 7 years 
Transportation means                                       up to 9 years 
Electronic and other equipment                      up to 4 years 
 
During the reporting period, the Group assesses whether there is any indication that the 
value of a tangible fixed (other than real estate) asset has been impaired. If any such 
indication exists, the Group estimates the recoverable amount of the tangible fixed asset. 
When the book value of a tangible fixed asset exceeds the recoverable amount, the Group 
forms a provision for the impairment, in order for the book value of the tangible fixed asset 
to reflect its recoverable value. The profit and losses from the sale of tangible fixed asset 
are determined based on their book value and are taken into account at the preparation of 
the pre-tax financial results. 
 
 
2.17 Real estate investments 
Investments in Real estate include fields and buildings owned by the Company for rental or 
for capital appreciation and are classified as investment properties. Initially, investments in 
real estate are registered at their acquisition value, which also includes the acquisition cost.  
After the initial registration, investments in real estate are measured at their fair value as 
determined by independent appraisers. Any changes are recognized in the statement of 
financial results.  
The assets are recognized until their sale or earlier, on the date that the Company ceases 
to have future economic benefits from the asset. In case of change of the use, such as 
owner occupation, they are transferred to the Tangible Fixed Assets category. The profit or 
loss arising is recognized in the statement of financial results of the reporting period.
